# 00_preamble — Claude_Task_Plan.md history archive

**HISTORY ONLY — NOT INSTRUCTIONS.** Nothing in this file binds any routine, and no routine needs to read it to do its job. Each entry is the full original text of a `Claude_Task_Plan.md` paragraph as it stood on 2026-10-30, kept for its incident evidence and reasoning; the plan now carries a condensed, rules-only version that points here. Where the two differ, the plan wins. Superseded, retired or since-corrected instructions appear here exactly as they were written; never execute them from this file.

## H-PRE-01 — BigQuery (Google Cloud MCP connector) — the canonical operational data substrate (project stocktrading498512)

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 61) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

- **BigQuery (Google Cloud MCP connector) — the canonical operational data substrate (project `stock-trading-498512`).** As of the 2026-06-06 cutover, all migrated state + history lives in BigQuery, NOT in repo `.md` files: positions/fills/marks (`events.*` → `state.current_positions`, `perf.strategy_daily`, `perf.kill_flags`), decisions (`events.decision_log` + `analytics.find_precedents()`), regime/router (`state.current_regime`), queues (`state.open_queue` / `events.queue_events`), per-strategy NAV + 2%-sizing base (`analytics.strategy_nav`), §13 reconciliation (`analytics.account_reconciliation`), calibration (`analytics.calibration_summary`), macro (`events.macro_series`), and the consolidated `state.daily_briefing`. Routines READ via `execute_sql_readonly` and WRITE via `execute_sql`. **Every routine MUST have the Google Cloud BigQuery connector enabled** — without it the routine cannot read or write state. Full source map + read/write override: Operating_Protocols.md §14 + §15.
- **Repo `.md` files (read/write) — now SPEC + cadence-working files only.** Spec/rules files (Strategy.md, Experiment_Parameters.md, Operating_Protocols.md, Claude_Task_Plan.md, AI_Trading_Foundation.md, B_Sub_Pattern_Taxonomy.md, the C/E methodology docs, HF_Resource_Catalog.md) are read for rules and edited only when a protocol/spec changes. Cadence-output working files (Daily.md, Weekly_*.md, Monthly_*.md, Quarterly_*.md, Annual_*.md) + the Strategy-A queue (Watchlist.md) are overwritten/edited per run. The former live-state + archive `.md` (Decision_Log, Portfolio_Ledger, Regime_State, the Pending_* queues, all archives) are **RETIRED — read/write BigQuery instead** (§15).
- **Calendar MCP** — narrowly scoped (2026-07-09: operator confirmed IBKR's own order-creation notification + `alert_emailer.gs` are sufficient for the ordinary case, so the calendar is no longer the default channel — see **Calendar MCP usage** below for the two surviving cases: a non-craftable/manual-entry order, and a BigQuery-unreachable pre-flight). Claude-only analysis (thesis construction, re-screens, research-deferral checkpoints, foundation-change assessments, constraint-relaxation reviews, router reviews) is NEVER on the calendar: it runs in-session or via the autonomous analysis queue (`state.open_queue` over `events.queue_events`, drained daily by D2). Structured adversarial reviews are likewise queue-driven via `events.queue_events` (queue `PENDING_REVIEW`; see ADVERSARIAL REVIEWS section).
- **IBKR connector (MCP)** for direct, authenticated access to the human operator's live brokerage account and market data. Crafts click-to-confirm order instructions (`create_order_instruction` → deep link), reads live account state (`get_account_summary` / `get_account_positions` / `get_account_balances` / `get_account_orders` / `get_account_trades`), and reads market data (`get_price_snapshot` / `get_price_history` / `search_contracts`). This connector replaces operator-typed order blocks (orders are now crafted and tap-confirmed) and operator screenshots (fills/positions/cash are read directly). Full protocol: Operating_Protocols.md §11 and the **IBKR connector usage** subsection below. Order-craft covers Equity/ETF plus single-leg Options and OPT–OPT combos/spreads (self-improvement audit ITEM 13, 2026-07-11); only mixed-type/FOP combos fall back to a manual text order block.
- **Web research tools** (Tavily, web_search, web_fetch) for deep-research cadences.

## H-PRE-02 — THE FAILURE MODE IS A WRONG ANSWER, NEVER AN ERROR — which is the whole reason this needs a rule. On a trunca

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 71) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

- **THE FAILURE MODE IS A WRONG ANSWER, NEVER AN ERROR — which is the whole reason this needs a rule.** On a truncated history `git log -S`/`-G`, `git log -L`, `git blame`, `git log --follow`, `git log --since`/`--until`, `git rev-list --count`, `git describe` and `git merge-base --is-ancestor` all run cleanly, exit 0, and resolve every pre-boundary change onto the boundary commit — `log -L`/`blame` printing a `--- /dev/null` header exactly as they would for a file genuinely created there. Two live misreadings are on record, both evidence-shaped, both false: OPS0's 2026-08-03 run had `--is-ancestor` return FALSE for `dcf92b5` / `9c52cfab` / `7beef36`, all three genuinely on `main`, and raised a false `unlanded_completed_run`; SL2's 2026-09-08 run concluded an **M2** commit had landed the pre-mortem SUPERSESSION BANNER fix when that commit was merely the boundary, and was refuted only because the session happened to think of checking clone depth. Nothing downstream catches either one: a confident, sourced, WRONG attribution written into `events.decision_log`, an `ops.alerts` message or a pinned rule is indistinguishable from a correct one.
- **A DEEPEN NOW RUNS BEFORE YOUR FIRST TOOL CALL, SO THE USUAL STATE IS COMPLETE — AND YOU STILL ASK, BECAUSE IT IS BEST-EFFORT.** `.claude/session-start.sh` (the SessionStart hook, §Branch and state propagation → "Session start") runs `git fetch --unshallow origin` when, and only when, `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` says the clone is shallow. Measured 2026-09-08 against this repo at 1390 commits: ~4.7 s, `.git` growing ~5.6 MB → ~26 MB. That is the routine container's own network and **not** a GitHub Actions runner, so it bills no Actions minutes and CLAUDE.md's push-batching economics do not apply to it. The hook never blocks session start, so a deepen defeated by a network blip or by GitHub rate-limiting leaves the clone exactly as truncated as it was, having printed one WARNING line to a stream nothing gates on. **Its success is therefore the common case, not a guarantee — the precheck below is what turns it into one.**
- **THE HISTORY-DEPTH PRECHECK — three steps, in this order, before the FIRST history-reasoning command of any run that will assert an authorship, an "introduced in", a "has not changed since", or an ancestry verdict.** Do NOT collapse them into a single "try to deepen, bail on any error" test: the two failure modes are opposite and conflating them is how the skip path gets armed on a healthy clone. **1. ASK** — `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository`. `false` means the history is complete; **skip step 2 entirely and proceed — do NOT run the `--unshallow` fetch at all.** **2. DEEPEN (only when step 1 printed `true`)** — `git fetch --unshallow origin`. **3. RE-ASK** — `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` again; `false` means the deepen worked. **`fatal: --unshallow on a complete repository does not make sense` is NOT evidence of an undeepenable clone — it is proof of the OPPOSITE** (it appears only when step 1 was misread and step 2 was run on a complete repo), so treat it as complete and proceed. Step 1 is local, needs no network, and on a session the hook already deepened it returns `false` and costs nothing further — there is no run cheap enough to justify skipping it.
- **UNDEEPENABLE (step 3 still prints `true`) → every history answer this run is INCONCLUSIVE, never a finding.** Do not write an attribution, a "first landed in", a "never changed since", or a non-ancestor verdict into `events.decision_log`, `ops.alerts`, a pinned rule, a cadence `.md` or a run `<note>` on truncated evidence. Write what is actually true instead — `history truncated at <boundary>; attribution not established this run` — and leave the question open for a session that can answer it. This is the **State provenance** rule (§Shared rules referenced across prompts) applied to git: an answer read off a truncated history is an INFERENCE wearing a measurement's clothes, and the absence of a pre-boundary commit is exactly the "inference drawn from the ABSENCE of a record" that rule calls the highest-risk kind. Routines whose own steps depend on this — OPS0's STEP 4 landing sweep most of all — carry their OWN consequences for the undeepenable case; read them there, not here.
- **THE TELL, for a session that has already reasoned before reading this.** Several independent `-S`/`-L`/`blame` probes all landing on the SAME commit, or a `--- /dev/null` header on a file you know predates it, is the shape of a boundary, not of an author. That is precisely the shape SL2's first pass produced. Stop and run step 1 before writing the conclusion down.

| **M1b** (strategy mapping) | `02_regime_router.md` + `03–07` (mapping needs the activation rules) | — |
| **W3 / W4 / M4 / Q4 / A3 / D1 / AR_orchestrator** (multi-strategy) | the slices for the strategies in scope (+ `08_pre_mortems.md` — for reviews, and for **M4** additionally as an EXECUTED surface: §M4 section I walks its owner-assigned loci every fire, 2026-09-01. **This row grants READ ACCESS ONLY and is not evidence that any routine on it performs any check.** Qualifier widened 2026-09-01 because the bare "for reviews" wording was read as M4's read-scope declaration and used to justify seven `Owner: M4` assignments across Strategy C and Strategy E, while M4 "does not invoke any review prompt — it only enqueues" and, until section I, touched the file nowhere. Do not infer an owner from this row — read the routine's own section for a step that reads the surface, per the OUT-OF-SCOPE FINDINGS rule's verified-consumer clause) | — |
| **A1 / A2** (annual foundation sweep + constraint audit) | `01_shared_regime_vocabulary.md` + `08_pre_mortems.md` (the pre-mortems carry the per-strategy foundation-citation graph both routines parse); pull an individual `03–07` slice only for a specific per-strategy question | `Strategy.md` whole — ~366 KB would exhaust the context budget before research begins |

## H-PRE-04 — Session end — explicit ownPR publication. (1) Commit the routine's output — every output commit's SUBJECT LEAD

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 111) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

**Session end — explicit own-PR publication.** (1) Commit the routine's output — **every output commit's SUBJECT LEADS with `<ROUTINE> <YYYY-MM-DD>: ` (the routine id, then its Denver run date; for a routine hosted inline by OPS2, the HOSTED routine's id)** (added 2026-10-26, W5 SPEC-DEFECT NOTICE INTAKE hosted by OPS2, closing OPS0's `routine_landing_subject_omits_marker_convention`, alert `a0174612-65ed-4be2-940d-da0d7692e575`): `scripts/write_routine_commit_markers.sh` derives the `ops.routine_commit_markers` row from the commit SUBJECT alone (`marker_routine_from_subject`), so a free-prose subject lands the output but writes no marker, which silently disables the §38 self-heal and OPS0 STEP 4(f)'s marker pre-check for that run — and for a routine with no exclusive output path (D3, SL2, SL5) produces a FALSE `unlanded_completed_run` (measured: W1 2026-09-27, commit `96d42f1`, subject led with prose; the PR TITLE convention below does not cover the commit subject). Halt records keep the RUNBOOK §48 convention instead. Then explicitly `git push -u origin <assigned-branch>`, and verify `git ls-remote --exit-code origin refs/heads/<assigned-branch>` equals the local `HEAD` SHA. Retry push verification up to three total attempts with short backoff (~5s, then ~15s). (2) Reuse an OPEN PR only when its head repository and exact branch are this repository and this assigned branch, with base `main`; otherwise create that PR, e.g. `gh pr create --base main --head <assigned-branch> --title '<ROUTINE> <YYYY-MM-DD>: <summary>' --body '<output and validation>'`. Record the selected PR number and exact head SHA. Do not select by title, age, or an arbitrary first PR. (3) Fetch current `main`; it must be an ancestor of the PR head. If main advanced, merge `origin/main` into ONLY the assigned branch, resolve only unambiguous conflicts, regenerate affected slices, rerun the local gates, push without force, and restart exact-head verification. On ambiguity, leave the branch/PR intact and report the conflict. (4) Wait for the LATEST results at that exact head: **`CI required`, `Security required`, `Dependency review required`, and native `CodeQL` must all complete successfully**. Inspect check-runs/statuses for that SHA, including the native code-scanning check; an old successful run, a green aggregate from another SHA, pending/missing/skipped/cancelled checks, or `gh pr checks --required` alone when it omits native CodeQL is insufficient. Verify the three named workflow checks originate from GitHub Actions. Re-read the PR head and current main before dispatch; if either changed, repeat the ancestry/check loop. A content failure is repaired on this OWN branch and gets a new checked head; do not blindly rerun a deterministic failure. (5) Dispatch exactly `gh workflow run auto-merge-claude.yml --repo JackOfSpade/Stock-Trading --ref main -f pr_number=<selected-number>`. No branch sweep follows; after a confirmed merge that workflow deletes only this PR's own head branch, and only while it still names the merged head commit. (6) Poll that selected PR, fetch `origin/main`, and require GitHub's `merged=true` plus its recorded `merge_commit_sha` AND the selected checked head to be ancestors of `origin/main` before declaring landing. The dispatch response only confirms acceptance; it is not a merge receipt. If the workflow rejects stale main/head/checks, repeat the own-branch update/check path rather than bypassing it. Bound the publication wait to the session's remaining time, preserve the PR on timeout, and report its URL, exact SHA, outstanding checks and whether landing was confirmed. Only confirmed main landing satisfies the repo-output terminal completion gate; a queued check, accepted dispatch, or verified branch push alone does not.

The dispatch response only confirms acceptance; it is not a merge receipt. If the workflow rejects stale main/head/checks, repeat the own-branch update/check path rather than bypassing it. Bound the publication wait to the session's remaining time, preserve the PR on timeout, and report its URL, exact SHA, outstanding checks and whether landing was confirmed. Only confirmed main landing satisfies the repo-output terminal completion gate; a queued check, accepted dispatch, or verified branch push alone does not. *(Pre-2026-10-30 history and evidence for this rule: `ops/plan_history/00_preamble.md` § H-PRE-04.)*

## H-PRE-05 — A ZEROREPODIFF RUN PUSHES NOTHING, AND ITS completed IS NOT THEREBY UNGATED — SAY SO IN <note (added 20260921,

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 113) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

**A ZERO-REPO-DIFF RUN PUSHES NOTHING, AND ITS `completed` IS NOT THEREBY UNGATED — SAY SO IN `<note>` (added 2026-09-21, W5 SPEC-DEFECT NOTICE INTAKE, closing AR_att's `session_end_push_unstated_for_zero_diff_run`, alert `0b660d32-9e31-4c99-91df-9e15fdacbd2a`).** This section and the verified-push gate below were written for runs that produce repo output and said nothing about runs that legitimately produce none — AR_att and AR_orc on a no-op or BigQuery-only fire, M1R on its gate branch, and any routine whose entire durable output is BigQuery rows. Each such session was left to invent an answer, and the fleet has been logging `completed` on them under an unstated reading. **The reading is now stated: the gate protects the DURABILITY OF REPO OUTPUT, so where there is none it is satisfied vacuously.** Concretely — working tree clean AND `HEAD` equal to `origin/main` AND no commit made this run: do NOT push, do NOT create the branch on `origin`, and log `completed` normally. Pushing a bare branch pointer at `origin/main`'s own SHA lands no content: `ci.yml` has no branch or path filter, so it schedules a full CI run and consumes queue capacity with no durable gain. **What IS required is the affirmation**, in the terminal `<note>`, that the run wrote no repo file and therefore owes no push — one line, e.g. `REPO OUTPUT: NONE. Working tree clean at <sha>, equal to origin/main; no commit made, so no push is owed and the verified-push gate is satisfied vacuously, not skipped.` Without it, "wrote nothing" and "wrote something and failed to push it" are indistinguishable in `ops.run_log`, which is the exact ambiguity the gate exists to remove. **The converse still binds with full force:** ANY commit made this run — even one file, even a pure-prose edit — must complete the own-PR publication protocol and confirmed main landing before `completed`, per the retry ladder and failure wording below. Never read this clause as licence to skip a push you actually owe; the test is whether a commit exists, not whether the run "felt" like a no-op.

### Pass 2 input — q0030, 2026-10-30

_The newly removed second-pass text is retained in this verbatim input; history only._

Working tree clean at <sha>, equal to origin/main; no commit made, so no push is owed and the verified-push gate is satisfied vacuously, not skipped.` Without it, "wrote nothing" and "wrote something and failed to push it" are indistinguishable in `ops.run_log`, which is the exact ambiguity the gate exists to remove. **The converse still binds with full force:** ANY commit made this run — even one file, even a pure-prose edit — must complete the own-PR publication protocol and confirmed main landing before `completed`, per the retry ladder and failure wording below. Never read this clause as licence to skip a push you actually owe; the test is whether a commit exists, not whether the run "felt" like a no-op. *(Pre-2026-10-30 history and evidence for this rule: `ops/plan_history/00_preamble.md` § H-PRE-05.)*

## H-PRE-06 — NAMING A NEW mcp__ TOOL IN ROUTINE PROSE REQUIRES THE MANIFEST AND THE ALLOWLIST IN THE SAME COMMIT (added 202

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 117) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

**NAMING A NEW `mcp__*` TOOL IN ROUTINE PROSE REQUIRES THE MANIFEST *AND* THE ALLOWLIST IN THE SAME COMMIT (added 2026-08-30, after this stranded D1's 2026-08-30 run).** Second member of the same class as the slice rule above — a blocking `ci.yml` step that a routine can only fail *after* the session that could have fixed it has ended. `ci.yml` runs `check_connector_tools.py` and `check_settings_toolcov.py` as blocking steps, and BOTH scan `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (toolcov also scans `ops/triggers.json`; **neither scans `Operating_Protocols.md`, `Strategy.md`, `strategy/**` or `task_plan/**`** — naming a tool THERE trips nothing, which is itself a trap in the other direction: it means a green CI is not evidence that a tool named in those files is callable). Writing a `mcp__<Connector>__<tool>` token into `Claude_Task_Plan.md` therefore obliges two companion edits **in the same commit**: (1) `ops/connector_tools.yaml` — set that tool's `use:` to `required` (CHECK3 fails on `unused`/`optional`), and (2) `.claude/settings.json` — append `<settings_prefix><tool>` to `permissions.allow` (CHECK2 and the toolcov check both fail without it). **THE TRAP IS THAT THE MANIFEST ALREADY LISTS THE TOOL.** OPS1's TOOL-INVENTORY DRIFT CHECK auto-adds every vendor-new tool at `use: unused`, and `unused` is deliberately INERT — it grants nothing. So the manifest containing an entry for the tool, with a long researched `note:` on it, is NOT evidence the tool may be called; only `use: required` is. Reading the entry and concluding "it is already in the manifest" is exactly the inference that failed here. **MEASURED, not hypothetical:** D1's 2026-08-30 run recorded a genuine, well-evidenced finding (FMP `company/profile-symbol` returns a `marketCap` for symbols its `market-cap`/`batch-market-cap`/`shares-float` siblings deny) and wrote `mcp__FMP__company` into its own item 3 with neither companion edit. Both CI runs on `claude/epic-cray-0s1x7b` (`4be9d4a`, `0dc500a`) failed identically on `CHECK3: Claude_Task_Plan.md:723: routine text calls `mcp__FMP__company` (FMP) but the manifest declares `use: unused``, so that day's `Daily.md` never reached `main`. The prose was CORRECT; only the manifest and the allowlist were stale. **So: whenever an edit adds or changes an `mcp__*` token, run `python scripts/check_connector_tools.py` and `python scripts/check_settings_toolcov.py` yourself before pushing** — seconds each, no credentials, and the same cheapest-checks-in-the-suite argument as the two `split_*.py --check` runs above. **Promotion is hand-authored, never mechanical** (`ops/connector_tools.yaml`'s EDIT DISCIPLINE header): OPS1 may auto-ADD at `unused`, but moving a tool to `required` means a routine is now actually wired to call it, so the `use:` change and the prose that calls it belong in one commit, authored together.

## H-PRE-07 — Title: [Claude] Confirm order — <ticker <BUY/SELL.

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 173) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

- **Title:** `[Claude] Confirm order — <ticker> <BUY/SELL>`.
- **Time:** the order's best execution time — **07:00 MT pre-market on the order day** (30 min before the 07:30 MT open). Never adjusted for human availability or load.
- **Throughput:** no cap on how many order-confirmation events may share a slot. Never stagger or defer to "spread load."
- **Description:** the human-readable summary `SIDE QTY TICKER TYPE LIMIT TIF`, the explicitly-labeled manual-entry text order block (no `url` — the connector could not craft this order), and "Enter this order manually in IBKR, confirm at or after market open."
- **Time zone / "today":** the **authoritative trading-day source is `state.trading_day_today`** (`bigquery/09_market_calendar.sql`) — query it for `today` / `is_trading_day` / `last_trading_day` / `next_trading_day` (America/Denver, holiday- and weekend-aware off `events.market_holidays`, which W5 auto-extends from the FMP connector). Whenever a routine needs "today" to create/delete/filter a dated calendar event **or a queue `due_date`**, use `state.trading_day_today.today`. Do NOT use the assistant-context `currentDate` field (UTC-based; during evening MT it has already rolled to the next calendar day — this deleted a same-day order-confirmation event on the META convergence exit, 2026-05-27) and do NOT compute the date with local Bash (`date`) — both reintroduce exactly the drift `state.trading_day_today` exists to remove. **NARROW EXCEPTION — an `ORDER_STAGED` row's `due_date` (`entry_window_close`) is NOT `.today` (added 2026-09-10, after this bullet was traced as the instruction that produced the defect).** That one `due_date` is a DEADLINE ON A LIVE ORDER rather than a stamp of when the row was written, and it is the only `due_date` in the system attached to something that has to TRADE. Every Claude order is `DAY` (Operating_Protocols.md §11), so an instruction crafted AFTER the close reaches `state.trading_day_today.next_trading_day`, not `.today` — and D2/D2a craft post-close (~16:5x MT) as their NORMAL case. Stamping `.today` there writes a window that closed hours before the order existed: the order can never trade inside its own window, and D2a Step 0 then matches **(c) window closed → terminal `expired`** instead of **(b) window still open → re-craft under the same `item_key`**, silently dropping the persist-and-wait intent after a single session. Follow Operating_Protocols.md §11's `entry_window_close` rule instead: it must be **>= the session S the instruction being crafted will actually reach** (`.today` for an intraday craft, `.next_trading_day` for a post-close craft); if the intent's real deadline is earlier than S, do not craft at all — set the row terminal and log that. Measured live 2026-09-10: 8 of 67 post-close `pending` stagings between 2026-07-20 and 2026-09-09 carried an already-closed window. `state.staged_order_window_invalid` (`bigquery/233_staged_order_window_dead_on_arrival.sql`) now surfaces any such row and `ops.sp_sq_daily_staging_cap_check` raises `staged_order_window_dead_on_arrival` on it. **The exception is scoped to `queue='ORDER_STAGED'` ONLY** — every other queue lane (`PENDING_ANALYSIS`, `PENDING_DRAFT`, `PENDING_REGIME_REFRESH`, …) keeps `.today`, and W4 §C's **HARD CHECK** explicitly FORBIDS a next-trading-day default on its lane. The two rules point in opposite directions on purpose, because the deadlines mean different things: an analysis can be completed tonight, an order cannot be filled tonight. Do not generalize either one across lanes. Binds D2, D2a, D3, W4, M4, Q4, A1, A3.
- **Notification:** alarm fires at event-time so the human's only job is to enter and confirm.

### Pass 2 input — q0056, 2026-10-30

_The newly removed second-pass text is retained in this verbatim input; history only._

## H-PRE-08 — Connector preflight (FIRST — before runlogging, the dependency gate, or any routine's own Step 0). Before any

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 188) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

- **Connector pre-flight (FIRST — before run-logging, the dependency gate, or any routine's own Step 0).** Before anything else, prove the connectors this routine needs are live with one trivial liveness read each: **BigQuery** via `SELECT * FROM `stock-trading-498512.state.trading_day_today`` (every routine — this is also the `today` the templates below need, so it is near-zero extra cost); for **D1/D2** the **IBKR** connector via `get_account_summary`; and for the **order-STAGING routines** (D2, D3, M4, Q4, A1, A3, AR_orc — routines that may need to create a `[Claude] Confirm order` event for a non-craftable order, 2026-07-09: craftable Equity/ETF orders no longer need one — see Calendar MCP usage; D1 stages nothing, so it is exempt, **and W4 was REMOVED from this list 2026-09-13 for exactly the same reason** — W5 SPEC-DEFECT NOTICE INTAKE, adjudicating W4's `w4_calendar_preflight_contradicts_no_staging_boundary` notice. W4's own section states unconditionally that it stages no order of any kind and "must not … create a calendar confirmation" (NO W4 STAGING GATE EXISTS), and its BOUNDARY paragraph forbids it from calling Calendar MCP *at all*; so this bullet was ordering W4 to make a call its own section prohibits, to prove a connector live for an event it can never create. W4's only outputs are idempotent `PENDING_ANALYSIS` handoffs and Watchlist writes, which D2 converts. Nothing is lost by the removal: if a future revision ever gives W4 an order to stage, that revision must put it back here *and* lift the BOUNDARY prohibition — one without the other reproduces this contradiction) the **Calendar** connector via a 1-day `list_events` read. The point is to catch a de-authed/expired connector in seconds at the top of the run instead of mid-routine (the recurring owner-OAuth BigQuery de-auth — RUNBOOK §15/§26). A pre-flight failure is first routed through the **TRANSIENT-FAILURE WAIT-AND-RETRY** bullet below — only a non-waitable error (e.g. token-expired/re-auth) or an exhausted retry ladder takes the branches here. Then route the failure by which connector failed and whether the routine can proceed safely:
  - **BigQuery unreachable (token expired / re-auth required).** The alert sink is itself down, so you canNOT `sp_raise_alert`/write `ops.alerts`; the only first-class channel is a **`[Claude] ATTENTION — RE-AUTH BigQuery connector` calendar event — create it immediately.** Then branch on the routine: **D1 is research-only and stages no orders → proceed in DEGRADED MODE** (read book/marks from the IBKR connector, carry regime forward from the prior `Daily.md`, defer every BigQuery side-write, and banner `Daily.md` exactly as the 2026-06-26 run did). **D2/D2a/D3 require canonical state → HALT cleanly** (never run on missing/stale state; craft no orders). **D2a is named here explicitly rather than left to class-inference (added 2026-09-21, after the SECOND D2a slot had to derive it unaided — precedent 2026-08-23, commit `0f15df5`): its own section's `STEP 0 — BROKER RECONCILIATION RUNS UNCONDITIONALLY` invariant reads like an override of this branch and is not one — it is scoped to the TRADING-ENABLE GATE. See D2a's CONNECTOR-DOWN DISPOSITION limb, which also states what a halted slot still owes. **D2 carries its own CONNECTOR-DOWN DISPOSITION limb for the same reason (added 2026-09-21, hours later, by the D2 slot of the same incident): its TRADING-ENABLE GATE ends in a six-item "NOT BLOCKED, ever" carve-out list that is likewise scoped to a gate reading FALSE, and exactly one entry on it — `Watchlist.md` updates — is performable with no connector, which is precisely the one a halted session must not perform. Read the limb of the routine you are running; the three are not interchangeable.** **AR_att likewise carries its own CONNECTOR-DOWN DISPOSITION limb (added 2026-09-21, the fourth slot of the same outage) — and it closes a gap of a different shape from D2's and D2a's: this branch names neither half of the AR pair, while the scope sentence above binds "the adversarial attacker/orchestrator" to RUN the pre-flight, so AR_att was ordered to probe with no stated consequence for a failed probe. Its limb HALTS the routine and pins the trap peculiar to it — the no-op "No adversarial reviews due for attacker today." line is pure text, emittable with zero connectivity, and is the one output a halted fire must never produce. **n=4 in one outage (2026-09-21), and five more slots re-derived theirs on 2026-09-28: LANDED 2026-09-28 — see the FLEET CONNECTOR-DOWN DISPOSITION TABLE below, which gives EVERY routine a branch by class so the next routine reads its answer instead of deriving it.**** Also create NO second calendar event if an earlier routine already raised one this incident — amend that event in place with the blast-radius escalation, per INCIDENT INHERITANCE. **BUT THE THREAD HAS A HARD CAP AND CLIPS SILENTLY, SO CHECK BEFORE YOU AMEND (added 2026-09-21 by the AR_att slot, which measured it on the fourth append of that day's outage).** The Calendar API caps `description` at 8,192 characters and offers no append primitive: the Calendar update call replaces the field wholesale, so a multi-slot outage fills the thread and then clips each further append **on write, with no error the writing slot records**. It had already happened by the THIRD append on 2026-09-21: two independent reads returned a byte-identical truncation ending mid-clause, and the clipped block was D2's, which held the sole record of a ~$3.79k deferred park re-risk. **If the description ends mid-sentence, it is full: leave it intact and put your account in your own repo record instead** — the calendar carries the alert and the required operator action, the repo carries the account. Never rewrite ~8 KB to sign your name; a slot must not damage the incident thread in order to join it. The durable fix — a short, stable ACTION-REQUIRED banner on the event pointing at one per-incident repo file each slot appends to — LANDED 2026-09-28: see items (1) and (2) of the FLEET CONNECTOR-DOWN DISPOSITION TABLE below. The next-morning freshness + cadence dead-man's switches durably record the miss once BigQuery returns; resolve per RUNBOOK §26.

## H-PRE-09 — WHAT EVERY HALTED SLOT OWES (uniform; D1 in DEGRADED MODE owes (1) only and keeps its own deferredwrites reco

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 205) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

    - **WHAT EVERY HALTED SLOT OWES (uniform; D1 in DEGRADED MODE owes (1) only and keeps its own deferred-writes record).**
      1. **Calendar.** Create the `[Claude] ATTENTION — RE-AUTH BigQuery connector` event ONLY if none exists for this incident. Its description is a SHORT, stable ACTION-REQUIRED banner — re-authorize the connector in claude.ai connector settings, then RUNBOOK §26 — plus the path of the per-incident repo record. Later slots add at most one short `UPDATE hh:mm MDT <slot> halted` line and never rewrite the thread (the cap check above still governs).
      2. **Per-incident repo record.** The first detecting slot creates `ops/spikes/bigquery-deauth-<operating-date>-<slot>-halt-record.md` (operating-plane date, America/Denver, not UTC). Every later slot of the same incident APPENDS one `## <slot> slot, <date>: HALTED` section to that SAME file instead of creating its own. The commit subject follows the RUNBOOK §48 halt-record convention (it does not lead with the routine id).
      3. **No `ops.run_log` backfill after re-auth** — the freshness and cadence dead-man switches record the miss.
      4. **Never emit a "nothing due" / quiet-day line** — the queue and state are UNKNOWN, not empty.
      5. **Findings.** `ops.alerts` is unreachable, so never claim a finding was "filed"; put each under an `## Unfiled findings` heading in the incident record, one bullet each, naming the owning routine or surface. Readers: RUNBOOK §26 step 4, and OPS0's UNFILED-FINDINGS INTAKE.
      6. **The record's RECOVERY PROCEDURE names EVERY declared dependent of the halted routine (added 2026-10-11, W5 SPEC-DEFECT NOTICE INTAKE, closing SL3's `halt_record_replay_order_omits_catchup_safe_downstream`, alert `da0310fb-c7cd-46cb-bb55-4943089fb9b4`).** Both the replay-order list and the expected-signature list must cover every routine whose `depends_on` in `ops/cadence.yaml` names the halted routine, not only the ones that need a manual replay: a `catchup_safe` dependent with its own multi-day catch-up loop needs NO manual replay and is listed as such, with that reason stated ("own catch-up loop; recovers once the upstream's catch-up lands"). The 2026-09-21 D2a record listed D2 and D3 and omitted SL3 (correctly on the merits, but unstated), so SL3's own `halted` row and `missing_dependency` critical sat outside the stated expected signature and read as a fresh fault.
  - **IBKR unreachable (BigQuery up).** The documented `connector` hard-stop: `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert('critical', '<ID>', 'connector', 'IBKR connector unreachable at pre-flight', '<JSON>')`, log the run `'halted'`, and ABORT (D1/D2 cannot reconcile the book or craft orders without it). BigQuery is up here, so `ops.alerts` + `alert_emailer.gs` deliver this — no calendar event (2026-07-09; the RE-AUTH-BigQuery case above is the one pre-flight branch that still needs one).
  - **Calendar unreachable (BigQuery up; staging routines only).** Calendar is now needed only for a non-craftable order's manual-entry event (2026-07-09 — craftable Equity/ETF orders rely on IBKR's own notification instead, see Calendar MCP usage). A Calendar outage no longer blocks staging a craftable order: `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert('warning', '<ID>', 'connector', 'Calendar connector unreachable at pre-flight — craftable orders unaffected, non-craftable orders cannot be surfaced this session', '<JSON>')` (BigQuery is up here, so `ops.alerts` + `alert_emailer.gs` deliver it) and proceed. Only hard-stop (`CALL ops.sp_raise_alert('critical', ...)`, log the run `'halted'`, ABORT) if this session is actually about to stage a non-craftable order and cannot surface its manual-entry block any other way.

## H-PRE-10 — TRANSIENTFAILURE WAITANDRETRY (owner directive 20260718) — binds ALL routines; applies to EVERY external call

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 217) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

- **TRANSIENT-FAILURE WAIT-AND-RETRY (owner directive 2026-07-18) — binds ALL routines; applies to EVERY external call in the run** (the connector pre-flight above, every BigQuery query/DML, IBKR / Calendar / GitHub / web-fetch calls) and runs BEFORE any degrade/halt/alert branch elsewhere in this section is taken. Principle: **any error that time alone can plausibly fix is caught and waited against in-session**; only genuinely non-waitable errors — or waits that would outlive the session — fall through to the existing failure routing. (Born from the 2026-07-18 quota incident: D2a/AR_orc correctly alerted-and-halted on a daily-quota exhaustion, but no routine had a sanctioned way to ride out the *short*-transient class at all.)
  - **DIAGNOSE BY PROBE, ONCE PER INCIDENT (v2, 2026-07-18 adversarial review):** classification is by BEHAVIOR, not error text alone. On the first unexpected BigQuery-side failure, run ONE probe pair — `SELECT 1` (no table reference) and, only if ambiguity remains, a dry-run of the failing statement — then classify: `SELECT 1` also fails → connector/service down (auth wording → the RE-AUTH branch; otherwise waitable). `SELECT 1` PASSES while fresh table scans fail with "Custom quota exceeded"/`QueryUsagePerUserPerDay` → the **DAILY-QUOTA SIGNATURE** (RUNBOOK §2 addendum: metadata / dry-run / cached-repeat / 0-byte calls keep passing while every fresh scan fails, so a healthy-looking connector does NOT rule this out; check the per-user override first) → non-waitable. Passes + deterministic `invalid`/`notFound` on one statement → non-waitable code/schema error. Passes + intermittent failure → transient, enter the ladder. Do NOT re-run the probe pair at later rungs — once per distinct incident: every extra probe is another min-billed job in exactly the degraded windows where job count is least affordable (all four recorded quota exhaustions were job-count-driven, RUNBOOK §2).
  - **WAITABLE — retry in-session:** rate limits and short-window/concurrency quotas (BigQuery `rateLimitExceeded` / `jobRateLimitExceeded` / `quotaExceeded` on a concurrent or per-minute-class quota; HTTP 429/503), transient backend errors (`backendError` / `internalError` / 5xx / timeouts / dropped connections), an MCP connector call that errors or hangs WITHOUT an auth/permission message (IBKR gateway blips included), git push/fetch network errors (the "Session end" push-verification backoff is the pre-existing instance of this rule), and read-after-write visibility lag (a row you JUST wrote not yet visible — re-read it; never blind-re-write).
  - **NON-WAITABLE — route immediately per the existing branches, do NOT retry:** auth/permission failures (`accessDenied` / 401 / 403 / token-expired — the RE-AUTH pre-flight branch above), SQL syntax/schema errors (`invalid`, `notFound`), control-gate aborts from the trading-enable gate or an order-guard block (correct halts, not transients — never waited; an `sp_assert_deps` unsatisfied-DEPENDENCY abort is NOT handled by this ladder either, but it gets its own DEPENDENCY-WAIT WINDOW — see the Dependency-gate bullet below: ~10-min polls up to ~60 min, a separate budget from this ladder's cap), data-integrity failures (cash tripwire, dual-path disagreement), and **daily-window quotas**: `QueryUsagePerUserPerDay` resets only at midnight US/Pacific — hours beyond any session's life — and RUNBOOK §2's settled 2026-07-11 policy is that this cap must be NON-binding, so exhaustion is a policy violation to surface loudly (alert as D2a did 2026-07-18: `bigquery_quota_exhausted` — a LATCHING category under `ops.alert_policy`'s fail-closed allowlist, cleared by a human or by a later session's verified-clear rule in INCIDENT INHERITANCE below, NOT by the mechanical resolver) and halt/degrade per the routine's pre-flight branch; the next scheduled firing / OPS0 catch-up is the retry for that class.
  - **MCP-TRANSPORT REJECTION — THE ONE CARVE-OUT FROM "NON-WAITABLE", AND IT GETS A ZERO-WAIT RETRY (2026-09-11, closing the fleet-wide `mcp_arg_drop_rate` reports).** The BigQuery MCP tool can reject a WELL-FORMED call with the argument-shaped error **`Required parameter is missing: query`** when the `query` argument WAS supplied. Read literally, the bullet above sends that to "SQL syntax/schema errors (`invalid`, `notFound`) → route immediately, do NOT retry", and THE LADDER below has no zero-wait rung — so a routine applying this section by the letter would halt or degrade on a failure that ONE immediate verbatim re-issue clears. That is wrong, and it has bitten repeatedly: measured on at least **5 distinct operating dates** (2026-08-18 D1; 2026-09-07 OPS1; 2026-09-08 OPS1 4-of-18 + D2a; 2026-09-09; 2026-09-10 D3 3-of-11 and SL5 3-of-30), **21 distinct (routine, run_date) sightings in `ops.run_log`**, hitting readonly SELECTs, DML UPDATEs, `CALL`s and parameterless reads alike, with no argument difference between the failing and succeeding attempt. **THE RULE: an argument/parameter-shaped error returned by the MCP LAYER ITSELF, where no provider job was created — no `queryId`, no bytes billed, nothing in `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.JOBS_BY_PROJECT` — is WAITABLE, and takes ONE immediate verbatim re-issue BEFORE the ladder proper.** Re-send the identical call; do not rewrite it, do not shorten it, do not "simplify to isolate the problem" (each of those has produced a wrong diagnosis here). If the immediate re-issue succeeds, that is the whole incident: note it with `RETRY[kind=mcp_arg_drop;n=1;waited_s=0;outcome=recovered]` and carry on — **no alert, no degrade, no halt.** This carve-out does NOT relax the ladder for a genuine BigQuery rejection: a real `invalid`/`notFound` carries a `queryId` or a SQL-level message and stays non-waitable. **`WRITE SAFETY` above still binds and is what makes this safe on a write** — OPS1 proved the 2026-09-08 DML case never reached BigQuery by reading its effect back (`SELECT COUNT(*)` returned 0) before re-issuing, and the re-issue then landed exactly one row; verify the effect, then re-issue, never blind-re-write.
  - **IF THE IMMEDIATE RE-ISSUE ALSO FAILS, RETRY IT AGAIN — DO NOT INFER A SIZE LIMIT (corrected 2026-09-11; an earlier cut of this bullet asserted a ~13KB request ceiling and that was WRONG).** The drop is per-call, so two in a row is unremarkable: at the measured 22-27% per-call rate, two consecutive failures land about 5-7% of the time. **THERE IS NO DEMONSTRATED REQUEST-SIZE CEILING.** Measured against `events.adversarial_reviews`, single-call `CALL ops.sp_write_adversarial_review(...)` requests carrying `body_md` of **27,415 / 29,824 / 44,387 characters** all SUCCEEDED from AR_orc — a cloud routine whose only BigQuery path is this same MCP tool, with `p_body_md` a single STRING parameter and no chunking anywhere in its signature (bigquery/145, bigquery/146), so each was necessarily ONE request. Two of those landed 2026-09-10 00:50 and 00:57 UTC, inside AR_orc's logged run window, on the SAME operating date a ~16KB request was mistakenly diagnosed as over a ceiling. That diagnosis (`ops.alerts` `mcp_query_size_ceiling_measured`, D2 2026-09-10) rested on two consecutive failures of one large statement and is refuted by the rows above; the EARLIER refutation (`mcp_query_size_ceiling_unsupported`, AR_att 2026-09-09) was correct. **So: keep retrying the identical call, and never let request size become the explanation for this error.** If identical retries keep failing several times over, that is a connector incident, not a sizing problem — report it per the run-issue convention with the verbatim error and your request size, and leave the artifact INTACT. See the `<note>` rules in the Run-logging bullet below. **LOCALISED 2026-09-11 — see RUNBOOK §51 and `ops/spikes/bigquery-mcp-query-arg-drop-2026-09-11.md` for the full measurements.** Re-measured over **204 calls**: the drop runs **13.7%** and hits ONLY `execute_sql` / `execute_sql_readonly` — the connector's two list-tools failed **0 of 80** in the same windows, 20 of them interleaved call-for-call with failing SQL calls, so the fault is specific to the `query` argument rather than to the connector. It is NOT the model's emission (30 byte-identical tool_use blocks in ONE assistant message produced 25 successes and 5 rejections) and NOT positional (emitting `query` first still named `query` in all 10 failures). Independently re-confirming the no-ceiling finding above: byte-identical requests of **16,042** characters succeeded 6/8 and of **25,018** characters 3/4, and no response at any size mentioned size, length or a limit. **And the error is indistinguishable from genuine caller error** — calling the tool with NO `query` at all returns the byte-identical string — which is why this misdiagnoses so reliably, and why the message is filed with the vendor as a defect in its own right.
  - **SYNCHRONOUS-TIMEOUT HANDOFF — NOT A FAILURE AT ALL, AND RE-ISSUING IT IS THE ONE THING THAT CORRUPTS DATA (2026-09-13).** `execute_sql` / `execute_sql_readonly` can return **`{"jobComplete": false, "jobId": "job_..."}`** — no rows, no error, no message. That is a **success**: BigQuery accepted the statement, the job is RUNNING, and it WILL run to completion and commit whether or not this session ever looks at it again. The MCP tool simply stopped waiting (default synchronous window ~20 s). **THE RULE: poll `get_query_results` with that exact `jobId` until it returns `jobComplete: true`, then read the rows from that response. Do NOT re-issue the statement, and do NOT treat this as a transient to be fed to THE LADDER below.** On a SELECT a blind re-issue merely double-bills; **on an `INSERT` / `UPDATE` / `MERGE` / `DELETE` / `CALL` it is a genuine double-write**, because the first job is still live and will commit — the re-issue does not replace it, it duplicates it. **THIS IS THE EXACT OPPOSITE SIGNATURE TO THE MCP-TRANSPORT REJECTION above, and the two must never be conflated:** §51's arg-drop is diagnosed by *no provider job having been created* (no `queryId`, no bytes billed, nothing in `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.JOBS_BY_PROJECT`) and is cleared by an immediate verbatim re-issue; here a job demonstrably WAS created, its id is in your hand, and re-issuing is precisely wrong. If the job id is lost before polling, do NOT re-run the statement blind — apply `WRITE SAFETY` and read the effect back first (`SELECT COUNT(*)` / the target row), exactly as OPS1 did for the 2026-09-08 DML case. **This is a live path, not a hypothetical:** measured 2026-09-13 over the trailing 180 days, **867 of 54,522** MCP-labelled BigQuery jobs (**1.6%**) ran past 20 s, on **every single operating day** (7–95/day), p99 **26.3 s**, max **195.7 s**; over 60 days the affected statements include **565 `SCRIPT`** blocks (the `ops.sp_routine_start` / `ops.sp_auto_resolve_alerts` pre-flight every routine runs, mean 29.1 s), **239 `SELECT`**, and the mutating **12 `MERGE` / 9 `INSERT` / 6 `UPDATE` / 1 `DELETE`**. Verified end-to-end the same day: a deliberately-timed-out read returned `jobComplete:false` + `jobId`, and `get_query_results` on that id returned the complete result set. Full record: **RUNBOOK §52**.
  - **THE LADDER (bounded, same session):** attempt → wait ~60 s → retry → wait ~5 min → retry → wait ~10 min → final retry (4 attempts, ~16 min max per ladder; deliberately NO zero-wait immediate retry — it amplifies job count for near-zero gain, v2 adversarial review). Apply **±20% jitter** to each wait (a shared root cause hits several routines in the same evening window — 2026-07-18 precedent — and jitter desynchronizes their retries); when the error carries an explicit server retry hint (`Retry-After` / `retryDelay`), honor it instead, capped at 10 min. Implement waits with the Bash tool as `sleep <seconds>` **with an explicit `timeout` parameter covering the sleep** (an un-parameterized foreground command is killed at the ~2-min harness default; keep each call ≤ 600 s and chain two calls rather than exceeding it). **Session-wide cap: ~30 minutes of cumulative waiting across all ladders** — once spent, treat every further failure as non-waitable. A wait that would need to exceed the cap is non-waitable by definition. **Absolute clamp (shared with the DEPENDENCY-WAIT WINDOW): never wait past 23:30 America/Denver** — `run_date` is pinned at session start while `log_ts` keeps advancing, and completions pushed across midnight distort time-of-completion telemetry (the `analytics.routine_health_scorecard` metric is midnight-safe as of bigquery/89, but the operational day boundary still is what it is).
  - **WRITE SAFETY:** before re-issuing any DML / insert / order-affecting call whose first attempt FAILED, verify the attempt didn't actually land (query for the row; `get_account_orders` for IBKR) — when in doubt, retry the READ of its effect, not the write. `create_order_instruction` is never blind-retried (the staging-atomicity bullet below governs); one retry is allowed only after verifying no order was crafted.
  - **INCIDENT INHERITANCE (v2, 2026-07-18) — one incident, one alert thread.** (The strategy-blind pair M1a/M1R runs this, and its verified-clear converse, METADATA-ONLY and for `category = 'bigquery_quota_exhausted'` only, leaving `connector` rows to the non-blind routines — see the `ops.alerts` rule in M1a's Read access scope, added 2026-09-29.) When a waitable-class failure occurs AND `ops.alerts` is readable, FIRST check for an existing unresolved alert covering the same symptom class raised by an earlier routine: for LATCHING categories (anything absent from `ops.alert_policy`'s non-latching allowlist — the fail-closed default, which includes `connector` and `bigquery_quota_exhausted`) match ANY still-unresolved row regardless of age; for non-latching categories match only the last 24h. On a match: run ONE confirmation probe instead of an independent full ladder, carry `{"related_alert_id":"<id>"}` in the payload of anything you do raise, add note token `INCIDENT[ref=<alert_id>]`, and raise a NEW alert only if you add MATERIAL information (severity escalation, or a different blast radius — e.g. first order-staging routine blocked where the prior report was research-only). Never let inheritance downgrade a genuinely-new incident below `warning` — `info` is invisible to both the email and webhook relays. **Verified-clear (the converse):** if your pre-flight fresh table-scan liveness read SUCCEEDS and an open `bigquery_quota_exhausted` (or BigQuery-referencing `connector`) alert from an earlier window exists, you have just proven that condition cleared for a routine principal — resolve it, scoped: `UPDATE ops.alerts SET resolved=TRUE, resolved_ts=CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(), resolved_note='verified clear: fresh table scan succeeded, <ID> pre-flight <ts>' WHERE alert_id='<id>' AND resolved=FALSE`. These two BigQuery-infra categories ONLY — every capital-affecting class stays human-only per the fail-closed allowlist, and an IBKR/Calendar `connector` alert is NEVER cleared by a BigQuery probe.
  - **OUTCOME LOGGING:** a recovered retry changes nothing in status — log the run normally and record the narrative in `sp_routine_end`'s `<note>` (`transient <kind> x<n>, waited ~<m>m, recovered`; no new `ops.run_log` status values), and append the machine-parseable token **`RETRY[kind=<slug>;n=<attempts>;waited_s=<total>;outcome=recovered|exhausted]`** — one token per distinct incident, exact grammar with the closing bracket (`state.retry_telemetry`, bigquery/88, parses only well-formed tokens via REGEXP_EXTRACT_ALL; a malformed token is silently ignored, which wastes the signal). An exhausted ladder falls through to the EXISTING failure path (Failure-alerts bullet, `'failed'`/`'halted'`), with the alert text stating retries were already exhausted — so a human doesn't hand-retry what the session already waited out — and the ladder summarized in `error_msg`/`<note>`.
  - Existing narrower retry rules (the "Session end" push-verification backoff, AR_orc's echo-suspect cool-off, order-guard next-run re-evaluation, D3's no-retry on trigger-create) are unchanged and take precedence in their own scopes.

That is a **success**: BigQuery accepted the statement, the job is RUNNING, and it WILL run to completion and commit whether or not this session ever looks at it again. The MCP tool simply stopped waiting (default synchronous window ~20 s). **THE RULE: poll `get_query_results` with that exact `jobId` until it returns `jobComplete: true`, then read the rows from that response. Do NOT re-issue the statement, and do NOT treat this as a transient to be fed to THE LADDER below.** On a SELECT a blind re-issue merely double-bills; **on an `INSERT` / `UPDATE` / `MERGE` / `DELETE` / `CALL` it is a genuine double-write**, because the first job is still live and will commit — the re-issue does not replace it, it duplicates it. **THIS IS THE EXACT OPPOSITE SIGNATURE TO THE MCP-TRANSPORT REJECTION above, and the two must never be conflated:** §51's arg-drop is diagnosed by *no provider job having been created* (no `queryId`, no bytes billed, nothing in `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.JOBS_BY_PROJECT`) and is cleared by an immediate verbatim re-issue; here a job demonstrably WAS created, its id is in your hand, and re-issuing is precisely wrong. If the job id is lost before polling, do NOT re-run the statement blind — apply `WRITE SAFETY` and read the effect back first (`SELECT COUNT(*)` / the target row), per WRITE SAFETY. Full record: **RUNBOOK §52**.
  - **THE LADDER (bounded, same session):** attempt → wait ~60 s → retry → wait ~5 min → retry → wait ~10 min → final retry (4 attempts, ~16 min max per ladder; deliberately NO zero-wait immediate retry — it amplifies job count for near-zero gain, v2 adversarial review). Apply **±20% jitter** to each wait (jitter desynchronizes retries of a shared root cause); when the error carries an explicit server retry hint (`Retry-After` / `retryDelay`), honor it instead, capped at 10 min. Implement waits with the Bash tool as `sleep <seconds>` **with an explicit `timeout` parameter covering the sleep** (an un-parameterized foreground command is killed at the ~2-min harness default; keep each call ≤ 600 s and chain two calls rather than exceeding it). **Session-wide cap: ~30 minutes of cumulative waiting across all ladders** — once spent, treat every further failure as non-waitable. A wait that would need to exceed the cap is non-waitable by definition. **Absolute clamp (shared with the DEPENDENCY-WAIT WINDOW): never wait past 23:30 America/Denver** — `run_date` is pinned at session start while `log_ts` keeps advancing, and completions pushed across midnight distort time-of-completion telemetry (the `analytics.routine_health_scorecard` metric is midnight-safe as of bigquery/89, but the operational day boundary still is what it is).
  - **WRITE SAFETY:** before re-issuing any DML / insert / order-affecting call whose first attempt FAILED, verify the attempt didn't actually land (query for the row; `get_account_orders` for IBKR) — when in doubt, retry the READ of its effect, not the write. `create_order_instruction` is never blind-retried (the staging-atomicity bullet below governs); one retry is allowed only after verifying no order was crafted.
  - **INCIDENT INHERITANCE (v2, 2026-07-18) — one incident, one alert thread.** (The strategy-blind pair M1a/M1R runs this, and its verified-clear converse, METADATA-ONLY and for `category = 'bigquery_quota_exhausted'` only, leaving `connector` rows to the non-blind routines — see the `ops.alerts` rule in M1a's Read access scope, added 2026-09-29.) When a waitable-class failure occurs AND `ops.alerts` is readable, FIRST check for an existing unresolved alert covering the same symptom class raised by an earlier routine: for LATCHING categories (anything absent from `ops.alert_policy`'s non-latching allowlist — the fail-closed default, which includes `connector` and `bigquery_quota_exhausted`) match ANY still-unresolved row regardless of age; for non-latching categories match only the last 24h. On a match: run ONE confirmation probe instead of an independent full ladder, carry `{"related_alert_id":"<id>"}` in the payload of anything you do raise, add note token `INCIDENT[ref=<alert_id>]`, and raise a NEW alert only if you add MATERIAL information (severity escalation, or a different blast radius — e.g. first order-staging routine blocked where the prior report was research-only). Never let inheritance downgrade a genuinely-new incident below `warning` — `info` is invisible to both the email and webhook relays. **Verified-clear (the converse):** if your pre-flight fresh table-scan liveness read SUCCEEDS and an open `bigquery_quota_exhausted` (or BigQuery-referencing `connector`) alert from an earlier window exists, you have just proven that condition cleared for a routine principal — resolve it, scoped: `UPDATE ops.alerts SET resolved=TRUE, resolved_ts=CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(), resolved_note='verified clear: fresh table scan succeeded, <ID> pre-flight <ts>' WHERE alert_id='<id>' AND resolved=FALSE`. These two BigQuery-infra categories ONLY — every capital-affecting class stays human-only per the fail-closed allowlist, and an IBKR/Calendar `connector` alert is NEVER cleared by a BigQuery probe.
  - **OUTCOME LOGGING:** a recovered retry changes nothing in status — log the run normally and record the narrative in `sp_routine_end`'s `<note>` (`transient <kind> x<n>, waited ~<m>m, recovered`; no new `ops.run_log` status values), and append the machine-parseable token **`RETRY[kind=<slug>;n=<attempts>;waited_s=<total>;outcome=recovered|exhausted]`** — one token per distinct incident, exact grammar with the closing bracket (`state.retry_telemetry`, bigquery/88, parses only well-formed tokens via REGEXP_EXTRACT_ALL; a malformed token is silently ignored, which wastes the signal). An exhausted ladder falls through to the EXISTING failure path (Failure-alerts bullet, `'failed'`/`'halted'`), with the alert text stating retries were already exhausted — so a human doesn't hand-retry what the session already waited out — and the ladder summarized in `error_msg`/`<note>`.
  - Existing narrower retry rules (the "Session end" push-verification backoff, AR_orc's echo-suspect cool-off, order-guard next-run re-evaluation, D3's no-retry on trigger-create) are unchanged and take precedence in their own scopes. *(Pre-2026-10-30 history and evidence for this rule: `ops/plan_history/00_preamble.md` § H-PRE-10.)*

## H-PRE-11 — This mechanically clears a small, explicit allowlist of critical/warning alerts whose truth is a recheckable f

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 237) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

This mechanically clears a small, explicit allowlist of critical/warning alerts whose truth is a re-checkable fact (`missing_dependency`, `missed_run`, `routine_stalled`, the `staleness` echo — see `ops.alert_policy`, fail-closed: every other category, including every capital-affecting class like `cash_tripwire`/`order_guard_block`/`trading_halted`, is untouched and stays human-only, drilled monthly via `ops.sp_fire_drill_alert_latch`). Running it here — before `state.trading_enabled`/`_mechanical` is ever read by a staging routine, and before this routine's own dependency gate below — means a stale alert from a prior day's transient (e.g. a stranded upstream that has since caught up) self-clears on the very next routine to run, instead of requiring a human `UPDATE ops.alerts`. Live incident this closes (verified 2026-07-06/07): a stranded D1 left `missing_dependency`/`missed_run`/`staleness` criticals open that would otherwise have kept `state.trading_enabled = FALSE` forever after D1/D2 caught up. **Mechanical backstop (self-improvement audit 2026-07-17):** the two trading-enable gate procedures (`ops.sp_assert_trading_enabled` / `ops.sp_assert_trading_enabled_mechanical`) now call this same `sp_auto_resolve_alerts()` internally, best-effort, as their FIRST statement — so even if THIS preamble call is skipped or runs before the routine's own freshness-making ingest, the gate itself clears a just-healed self-healing critical before deciding to halt. This closes the 2026-07-17 incident where D2a reached its gate at 16:22 before its preamble auto-resolve ran and halted trading for the whole day on an already-healed `staleness` echo (root SL5 `missing_dependency` had resolved at 00:30; marks were fresh by ~16:18). The resolver's only mechanical (non-agent) path — the nightly `cadence_check` scheduled query — was not yet live at the time of this incident: its console body was re-pasted to the `CALL ops.sp_sq_cadence_check()` wrapper on 2026-07-17 (OWNER_ACTIONS.md item B, verified live via `bq show --transfer_config`), and even so it runs only nightly (05:15 UTC), so a mid-day heal still waits up to ~24h for it. That is why the per-routine best-effort call was effectively the sole timely path, and its miss cost a full trading day — which the gate-internal backstop above now covers on every gated-routine run.

## H-PRE-12 — PREREFRESH HALT DISPOSITION — a gate that is FALSE only because today's marks are not in yet is NOT an incide

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 239) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

- **PRE-REFRESH HALT DISPOSITION — a gate that is FALSE only because today's marks are not in yet is NOT an incident (added 2026-09-01, `ops.alerts` `a5bb4024-887a-4ead-919f-6120088808cc`; `bigquery/206_staging_halt_prerefresh_disposition.sql`).** `state.trading_day_today.last_trading_day` equals TODAY from DENVER MIDNIGHT on a trading day, but `events.daily_marks` / `perf.strategy_daily` for today are written by D2a at 22:40 UTC — so `state.freshness.marks_fresh`/`engine_fresh`, and therefore `state.trading_enabled`, are FALSE for ~16.5h of EVERY trading day with `halt_reason` `state.freshness marks_fresh/engine_fresh not both TRUE`. D2 (23:15 UTC), D3 (00:45 UTC) and AR_orc (00:35 UTC) all run after D2a and never see it on a normal run; M4 (~15:22 UTC) and Q4 (20:00 UTC) see it EVERY period, and a catch-up run of any routine can see it too (the 2026-08-24 D2 case, `ops.alerts` `0efa2ef3` — the ONLY genuine instance in history before M4's). **D2a is deliberately NOT in scope**: it reads `state.trading_enabled_mechanical`, which carries no `marks_fresh`/`engine_fresh` term at all (`bigquery/176`), so a pre-refresh halt can never be its halt reason. **Q4 and A3 craft no orders by construction**, so for them `defer_to_craft_site` means they raise nothing at all — which is the whole point: today they would latch a capital-class critical for a halt that blocks nothing they do. Because `trading_halted` is capital-affecting and deliberately HUMAN-ONLY under the fail-closed `ops.alert_policy` allowlist, raising it at the gate costs an operator email and a manual `UPDATE ops.alerts` for a condition in which nothing is late and, usually, nothing was blocked — and worse, `ops.sp_raise_alert_once` dedups `trading_halted` on exact message text, so while one such row stays open EVERY later `trading_halted` raise fleet-wide (halt_all, cash_tripwire, order_guard_block, drawdown) is silently swallowed. **[CORRECTED 2026-10-13, owner directive, `bigquery/255_trading_halted_auto_resolve.sql`; closes W5 notice `97fa4b79`]** `trading_halted` is NO LONGER human-only: `ops.alert_policy` now lists it `latching = FALSE`, and `ops.sp_auto_resolve_alerts` Rule 7 closes every open `trading_halted` row once `state.trading_enabled` AND `state.trading_enabled_mechanical` both read TRUE with `halt_reason` NULL and no critical `missing_dependency` is open. So a CLEARED halt's row no longer sits open swallowing later raises. It cannot close a LIVE halt (any live cause makes a gate view FALSE), and it never touches the gate itself. Do NOT hand-close a `trading_halted` row the resolver would close on its own; `CALL ops.sp_auto_resolve_alerts()` instead. The defer-to-craft-site rule below is UNCHANGED and still owed: it avoids minting and emailing a halt that blocks nothing in the first place. **So at a TRADING-ENABLE GATE read, choose the alert by `state.staging_halt_disposition.gate_alert_action` instead of raising unconditionally:**

## H-PRE-13 — EMBEDDINGLAG REPAIR BEFORE ANY TRADINGENABLE READ (added 20260817, AR_orc) — BESTEFFORT, never gates; ONE sta

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 251) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

- **EMBEDDING-LAG REPAIR BEFORE ANY TRADING-ENABLE READ (added 2026-08-17, AR_orc) — BEST-EFFORT, never gates; ONE statement, not a retry ladder.** Every routine that reads `state.trading_enabled` / `state.trading_enabled_mechanical` / `state.b3_trading_enabled_check` must first `CALL ops.sp_embed_pending()` inside a best-effort wrapper (same shape as the Alert auto-resolve block above), then read the gate ONCE and treat that reading as authoritative. **Why:** `bigquery/107`'s CASE carries an unguarded `COALESCE(eh.is_healthy, FALSE)` term, so `halt_reason = 'state.embedding_health.is_healthy = FALSE'` fires the moment ANY `events.decision_log` row exists whose embedding chunk has not landed — and `ops.sp_log_decision` (`bigquery/116`:631-636; design note `bigquery/08_ops_procedures.sql`:10-14) commits the decision row FIRST and only then embeds, inside an exception handler that never rethrows. The unembedded window is therefore NOT sub-second: it lasts as long as the Vertex `ML.GENERATE_EMBEDDING` call takes, and on a swallowed embed error it lasts until the next opportunistic `sp_embed_pending()` (the next decision write, D2a's `sp_daily_refresh()`, or the ~06:00 UTC scheduled query). **Observed live 2026-08-17 18:40:53 MT** by AR_orc, ~57 s after an unrelated session's decision write: `state.trading_enabled` read FALSE on exactly this reason, and was TRUE again at 18:42:29 MT with `error_rows=0 / missing_rows=0 / dup_rows=0` — a clean self-heal, i.e. the FALSE was never a real halt. That matters because every gate site raises `sp_raise_alert_once('critical', …, 'trading_halted', …)` on FALSE and `trading_halted` is **human-latching with no auto-resolve rule anywhere**, so a read landing in that window leaves a permanent critical only a human can clear and declines an order craft that was never unsafe. `sp_embed_pending()` is the documented, idempotent, cheap-when-clean repair for exactly this state (`bigquery/02_ai_layer.sql` §`ops.sp_embed_pending()` (its `Incremental re-embedding` block; the quoted self-heal sentence is `bigquery/08_ops_procedures.sql`'s header) — "self-heals on the next `sp_embed_pending()` / `sp_log_decision()`"), so calling it first removes the routine's own write from the gate's inputs.
  - **This does NOT weaken the gate and is NOT the forbidden retry-on-a-control-gate.** It repairs a precondition the routine itself invalidated, then takes ONE reading — the same shape as, and sitting alongside, the 2026-07-17 mechanical backstop that already puts `sp_auto_resolve_alerts()` in front of this same read. `halt_all`, stale marks/engine, blocking criticals, reconciliation drift and a drawdown breach are all untouched by it, and a GENUINE embedding failure (Vertex actually down) still leaves `is_healthy = FALSE` after the repair call and still halts. Do **not** add a second read, a wait, or a backoff on top: the TRANSIENT-FAILURE ladder's NON-WAITABLE bullet still forbids waiting out a control gate. (D2's existing "re-read once" at its own gate is unchanged and remains correct for its own purpose — but note it re-runs only `sp_auto_resolve_alerts()`, which cannot move `state.embedding_health` at all, so it never covered this case.)
  - **DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED HERE — flagged for the owner / a W5 consolidation cycle.** The durable fix is one level down and is a live-SQL change to the trading-halt gate with fleet-wide blast radius: either fold this same `CALL` into `ops.sp_assert_trading_enabled` / `_mechanical` (`bigquery/85`:47-114) exactly as `sp_auto_resolve_alerts()` already is, or reconsider whether a DERIVED retrieval index belongs among a capital gate's halt terms at all. An AR_orc queue-drain fire is the wrong place to decide either unilaterally, so this revision hardens the READ SIDE in prose only and leaves the gate SQL untouched. Finding recorded in `events.decision_log` (`entry_type='ops-note'`, 2026-08-17) and alerted at `warning` under the existing `gate_ordering` category — deliberately NOT `critical`, since a critical would itself become a blocking-criticals term and halt trading over a report about a false halt.
  - **CORRECTION 2026-09-29 (interactive alert-triage session) — the "Why" above and BOTH sub-bullets above are SUPERSEDED BY EVENTS; the `CALL ops.sp_embed_pending()` instruction is RETAINED.** The owner took the second option the sub-bullet above names ("reconsider whether a DERIVED retrieval index belongs among a capital gate's halt terms at all") the SAME DAY this bullet landed: `bigquery/176_decouple_embedding_health_from_trading_gate.sql` (owner decision 2026-08-17, `events.decision_log` `f74c31be-9dbc-4af0-92b6-931d9ab21e3c`, closing the `gate_ordering` alert `816f7e25`) removed the embedding term from `state.trading_enabled`, `state.trading_enabled_mechanical` and `state.b3_trading_enabled_check`. MEASURED live 2026-09-29: none of the three view definitions references `embedding_health` or `is_healthy`. So an un-embedded decision row can NO LONGER make the gate read FALSE, a genuine embedding failure no longer halts, and nothing above is still "flagged for the owner". What an unhealthy embedding still does: `state.system_health.embeddings_healthy` reads FALSE, `find_precedents` retrieval degrades, and if `ops.sp_sq_daily_freshness_check` fires inside the window it raises the `critical` `staleness` alert — which `state.trading_enabled` EXCLUDES from `blocking_criticals` (`category NOT IN ('trading_halted', 'staleness')`), so it does not halt either. Keep calling `sp_embed_pending()` best-effort before a gate read — the owner kept it deliberately as harmless and useful (it keeps decisions promptly searchable) — but do NOT cite this bullet as evidence that embedding health gates trading, and do NOT treat an unhealthy embedding as a hard-stop (see the correction appended to the **Failure alerts** bullet below).

### Pass 2 input — q0074, 2026-10-30

  - **This does NOT weaken the gate and is NOT the forbidden retry-on-a-control-gate.** Do **not** add a second read, a wait, or a backoff on top: the TRANSIENT-FAILURE ladder's NON-WAITABLE bullet forbids waiting out a control gate. D2's own “re-read once” of `sp_auto_resolve_alerts()` remains unchanged.
  - `bigquery/176_decouple_embedding_health_from_trading_gate.sql` removed the embedding term from `state.trading_enabled`, `state.trading_enabled_mechanical` and `state.b3_trading_enabled_check`. An unhealthy embedding affects `state.system_health.embeddings_healthy` and `find_precedents` retrieval; `ops.sp_sq_daily_freshness_check` may raise `critical` `staleness`, which the trading gate excludes from `blocking_criticals` (`category NOT IN ('trading_halted', 'staleness')`). Keep calling `sp_embed_pending()` best-effort so decisions remain promptly searchable, but do NOT cite this bullet as evidence that embedding health gates trading, and do NOT treat an unhealthy embedding as a hard-stop (see **Failure alerts**).
