## H-PRE-14 — ROSTERCHANGE NOTICES (owner directive 20260804) — the six SISA transitions that MUST reach the operator's inb

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 256) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

- **ROSTER-CHANGE NOTICES (owner directive 2026-08-04) — the six SISA transitions that MUST reach the operator's inbox.** Strategy add/delete stays fully autonomous (no approval step, and none is being reintroduced — see the SISA note in CLAUDE.md); the owner is simply entitled to LEARN of every roster-membership change without having to query for it. These six categories — `strategy_shadow_registered`, `strategy_probe_registered`, `strategy_graduated`, `retirement_proposed`, `strategy_deregistered`, `roster_below_floor` — are raised at **`warning`**, never `info`: `alert_emailer.gs` (whose `SEVERITIES` array is `['critical', 'warning']`) and `scripts/alert_relay.py` both filter `info` out, so an `info` roster change is invisible to the operator BY CONSTRUCTION — the same reasoning that bumped `immediate_action_flagged` / `process_scorecard_signal` in consumption-closure CC-7, and the same principle the INCIDENT INHERITANCE bullet above states outright ("`info` is invisible to both the email and webhook relays"). `warning` is deliberate and verified safe: every trading gate (`state.trading_enabled` / `_mechanical`, `state.system_health.all_green`, `ops.sp_assert_trading_enabled*`) counts `severity = 'critical'` ONLY, so a roster notice can never contribute to a halt — do NOT raise these at `critical`, which would halt order staging on a healthy autonomous action. They are seeded non-latching in `ops.alert_policy` and auto-resolve once `notified_ts IS NOT NULL` (Rule 5, `bigquery/134_roster_change_notifications.sql`) — the notice clears itself the moment the email is actually delivered, so the board never accumulates them and no human `UPDATE` is ever needed. **EMAIL ONLY — these six are deliberately NOT pushed to ntfy (owner directive 2026-09-07: pushes are for things needing operator action).** `scripts/alert_relay.py` lists them in `NO_PUSH_CATEGORIES` and excludes them from its `alerts` query. Nothing about delivery weakens: the email is what this contract has always been about ("MUST reach the operator's inbox"), Rule 5 keys the auto-resolve on `notified_ts`, which only the EMAILER stamps, and the relay was never part of that loop. The `warning`-not-`info` rule above is unchanged and still load-bearing for the same reason — `info` is filtered out by `alert_emailer.gs`'s `SEVERITIES`, so an `info` roster change still reaches nothing. What changed is only that a completed, healthy, fully-autonomous roster action no longer buzzes a phone as though it were an incident, which is the same judgement `alert_emailer.gs` already encodes by rendering these in a non-fault lane rather than as "⚠ ALERT". **This list now lives in FOUR places** — here, `alert_emailer.gs`'s `ROSTER_NOTICE_CATEGORIES`, Rule 5's `IN` list, and `alert_relay.py`'s `NO_PUSH_CATEGORIES` — and `scripts/check_roster_notice_lockstep.py` (blocking, in `ci.yml`) asserts all four hold the SAME set, so the "a category in only one place is inert" hazard this contract used to carry unenforced is now machine-checked. Add a category to all four or to none. **Payload contract (render-critical — `alert_emailer.gs` v5's ROSTER CHANGE lane reads these exact keys, omits any that are missing, and never throws on a malformed payload):** `strategy_code`, `strategy_name`, `from_state`, `to_state`, `roster_active_before`, `roster_active_after`, `capital_usd` (dollars entering or leaving risk; `0` where none), `pct_nav`, `reason` (one line — the adoption thesis, or the decay/kill trigger), `git_commit`, `lifecycle_event_id`; add `kill_trigger` on any retirement or termination notice. Because the payload is enrichment and the message is not, the `<message>` argument MUST stand alone as a complete sentence naming the strategy and the transition — it is what renders if the payload is unreadable.

### Pass 2 input — q0075, 2026-10-30

_The newly removed second-pass text is retained in this verbatim input; history only._

- **ROSTER-CHANGE NOTICES (owner directive 2026-08-04) — the six SISA transitions that MUST reach the operator's inbox.** Strategy add/delete stays fully autonomous (no approval step, and none is being reintroduced — see the SISA note in CLAUDE.md); the owner is simply entitled to LEARN of every roster-membership change without having to query for it. These six categories — `strategy_shadow_registered`, `strategy_probe_registered`, `strategy_graduated`, `retirement_proposed`, `strategy_deregistered`, `roster_below_floor` — are raised at **`warning`**, never `info`: `alert_emailer.gs` (whose `SEVERITIES` array is `['critical', 'warning']`) and `scripts/alert_relay.py` both filter `info` out, so an `info` roster change is invisible to the operator BY CONSTRUCTION `warning` is deliberate and verified safe: every trading gate (`state.trading_enabled` / `_mechanical`, `state.system_health.all_green`, `ops.sp_assert_trading_enabled*`) counts `severity = 'critical'` ONLY, so a roster notice can never contribute to a halt — do NOT raise these at `critical`, which would halt order staging on a healthy autonomous action. They are seeded non-latching in `ops.alert_policy` and auto-resolve once `notified_ts IS NOT NULL` (Rule 5, `bigquery/134_roster_change_notifications.sql`) — the notice clears itself the moment the email is actually delivered, so the board never accumulates them and no human `UPDATE` is ever needed. **EMAIL ONLY — these six are deliberately NOT pushed to ntfy (owner directive 2026-09-07: pushes are for things needing operator action).** `scripts/alert_relay.py` lists them in `NO_PUSH_CATEGORIES` and excludes them from its `alerts` query. **This list now lives in FOUR places** — here, `alert_emailer.gs`'s `ROSTER_NOTICE_CATEGORIES`, Rule 5's `IN` list, and `alert_relay.py`'s `NO_PUSH_CATEGORIES` — and `scripts/check_roster_notice_lockstep.py` (blocking, in `ci.yml`) asserts all four hold the SAME set, so category drift is machine-checked. Add a category to all four or to none. **Payload contract (render-critical — `alert_emailer.gs` v5's ROSTER CHANGE lane reads these exact keys, omits any that are missing, and never throws on a malformed payload):** `strategy_code`, `strategy_name`, `from_state`, `to_state`, `roster_active_before`, `roster_active_after`, `capital_usd` (dollars entering or leaving risk; `0` where none), `pct_nav`, `reason` (one line — the adoption thesis, or the decay/kill trigger), `git_commit`, `lifecycle_event_id`; add `kill_trigger` on any retirement or termination notice. Because the payload is enrichment and the message is not, the `<message>` argument MUST stand alone as a complete sentence naming the strategy and the transition — it is what renders if the payload is unreadable. *(Pre-2026-10-30 history and evidence for this rule: `ops/plan_history/00_preamble.md` § H-PRE-14.)*

## H-PRE-15 — Run logging (every run) — BESTEFFORT, copy the template. Runlogging is observability and must never be able t

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 258) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

- **Run logging (every run) — BEST-EFFORT, copy the template.** Run-logging is observability and **must never be able to break the routine**, so wrap each logging CALL in a best-effort block and copy the template verbatim (don't hand-assemble the argument list). At the START:
  ```
  BEGIN
    CALL `stock-trading-498512.ops.sp_routine_start`('<ID>', <today (America/Denver, from state.trading_day_today)>,
      <session_id>, <branch>, '<the VERBATIM trigger instruction this session received>');
  EXCEPTION WHEN ERROR THEN SELECT @@error.message;  -- swallow: logging must not abort the routine
  END;
  ```
  **THE START ROW MUST COME FROM `sp_routine_start` WITH A NON-NULL INSTRUCTION — NEVER FROM A DIRECT `sp_log_run('…','started',…)` CALL (added 2026-10-04, W5 SPEC-DEFECT NOTICE INTAKE, closing AR_orc's `run_log_started_row_missing_instruction`, alert `82211222-7c00-43bc-a784-f4a0e53e256c`).** `sp_routine_start` is the only wrapper that populates `ops.run_log.instruction`; a `started` row with `instruction IS NULL` gives `state.routine_last_instruction` no sample for that run, so `state.instruction_drift` is blind to a drifted web-UI trigger — the same harm `run_log_start_row_missing` exists for, but on a row that DOES exist, so that detector never fires. MEASURED 2026-10-04: 16 of 425 `started` rows (3.8%) were NULL-instruction over 2026-08-01..09-10 across 7 routines; since 2026-09-11 only 4 of 247 (SL3 2026-09-13, 09-30, 10-01; SL2 2026-09-16) — all in the SL slots, whose sections defer to this preamble without restating the template, which is why this pin lives here. If a session finds it has written a NULL-instruction `started` row, repair it in-session with an `UPDATE` scoped to its own `run_id` (the `ops.sp_amend_run_note` precedent). **A post-hoc detector (a `state.*` view + `ops.sp_sq_cadence_check` raise block + `ops.alert_policy` row + SQ_VERSION bump) was deliberately NOT built:** it is a fleet-wide cadence-check rewrite for a defect measured at ~0.17/day and confined to two routines; revisit only if a fresh measurement shows the incidence rising again outside the SL slots.
  At the END (same wrapper), `'completed'` — or `'failed'`/`'halted'` + `error_msg` if the run aborted. **`error_msg` was WRITE-ONLY until 2026-09-08** — an exhaustive grep across `bigquery/`, `dbt/`, `scripts/`, `ops/*.gs` found no view, procedure, scheduled query, dbt model, python script or `.gs` file that ever read it, so a routine could log a perfect diagnostic and it reached nobody. **It now HAS a consumer:** `ops.sp_log_run` itself reads `error_msg` at write time (bigquery/230_run_outcome_notification.sql, see the amended Failure alerts bullet below) to build the `<detail>` text folded into a `routine_run_failed` (status `'failed'`/`'halted'`) or `routine_run_warning` (status `'completed'` with a non-blank `error_msg`) alert — falling back to `<note>` and then to a fixed literal only if `error_msg` is blank. Passing a real, specific diagnostic in `error_msg` — not a placeholder, not left blank — is now what actually reaches the owner's inbox for that row, on a `'completed'` run exactly as much as a `'failed'`/`'halted'` one. **Gate `'completed'` on a verified remote push** (see §Branch and state propagation → "Session end"): log `'completed'` only after `git ls-remote` confirms the assigned branch reached `origin`; otherwise log `'failed'`/`'halted'`. This makes `completed` in `ops.run_log` reliably imply the git output is on the remote rather than stranded on a reclaimed container (RUNBOOK §20):
  ```
  BEGIN
    CALL `stock-trading-498512.ops.sp_routine_end`('<ID>', <today>, 'completed', <session_id>, <branch>, <rows_written>, NULL, <note>);
  EXCEPTION WHEN ERROR THEN SELECT @@error.message;
  END;
  ```
  `<instruction>` is the exact text the web-UI trigger sent you (e.g. `'Read Claude_Task_Plan.md. Perform D2 — regular routine.'`); it lands in `ops.run_log.instruction` → `state.routine_last_instruction`, so the **live trigger text is verifiable by query** (no screenshots) and diffable against `scripts/print_routines.py`. **`<instruction>` is the trigger-of-record for drift detection, NOT a freeform task note:** always pass the **verbatim scheduled trigger** — even for an ad-hoc / one-off session that reuses an existing routine id (e.g. a one-time W5 §20/§21 remediation). Put what the one-off run actually did in `<note>` (the `sp_routine_end` arg) and the `session_id`, never in `<instruction>`. (`state.routine_last_instruction` only reads instructions of the canonical `Read Claude_Task_Plan.md. Perform …` shape, so a stray free-form note is ignored rather than mistaken for a drifted trigger — RUNBOOK §22 — but log the verbatim trigger anyway so the live read is faithful.) A run that never logs `completed` arms the freshness dead-man's switch (`state.freshness`), the **cadence dead-man's switch** (`state.cadence_watch` / `cadence_check.sql`), and the audit trail. (Data currency is *also* proven independently by `state.freshness.marks_fresh`/`engine_fresh`, which read the data tables directly and gate `all_green` — a missed log never hides stale data.) A routine becomes "monitored" automatically once it has logged a `completed` run in the last 14 days.

## H-PRE-16 — <note IS MANDATORY ON EVERY TERMINAL ROW. completed, failed and halted all require a real narrative note — wh

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 280) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

  **`<note>` IS MANDATORY ON EVERY TERMINAL ROW.** `completed`, `failed` and `halted` all require a real narrative note — what the run decided, what it measured, what it deliberately did not do. It is the ONLY durable account of a routine's reasoning: the session is gone afterwards, and no other artifact reconstructs why a no-op was the right answer. A no-op run needs a note *more* than a busy one, not less, because "nothing happened" and "the run was abbreviated and did not notice" look identical without it. This is now mechanically checked: `state.run_log_content_gaps` + `ops.sp_sq_cadence_check`'s `run_log_note_missing` warning (`bigquery/147_run_log_content_quality.sql`) flag any terminal row with a NULL or blank note. The check is deliberately note-only — a missing `<instruction>` is *not* alarmed, because it legitimately belongs on the paired `started` row (322 of 324 `completed` rows carry none). **NEVER SHORTEN A NOTE TO MAKE A FAILING WRITE SUCCEED (2026-09-11).** If a `sp_routine_end` / `sp_amend_run_note` / `sp_log_decision` / `sp_write_adversarial_review` call fails with **`Required parameter is missing: query`**, that is the MCP-TRANSPORT REJECTION handled in §Observability → TRANSIENT-FAILURE WAIT-AND-RETRY: **re-issue the identical call ONCE first.** Most of the time it simply lands. It is NOT evidence that your note is too long, and trimming it is the one response that is actually forbidden here — measured 2026-09-09, a session read that error on a 6495-byte note, inferred a size limit, rewrote the note down to 4703 bytes, and shipped a record abbreviated for a reason that did not exist (`ops.alerts` `mcp_arg_drop_guidance_not_at_point_of_use`). **For an adversarial-review transcript this is corruption, not abbreviation — and the checksum will NOT catch it:** `ops.sp_write_adversarial_review` ASSERTS the body SHA-256 at write time, but it hashes whatever the session hands it, so text shortened BEFORE the digest is computed yields a self-consistent row that passes every assertion. Verified 2026-09-11: `events.adversarial_reviews` is 193 rows with **0** hash mismatches — that table is intact, and equally that check could never have revealed this damage class. The only defence is not making the misdiagnosis. If the identical re-issue ALSO fails, retry it AGAIN — the drop is per-call and two in a row is expected a few percent of the time. **Do NOT conclude the note is too big: there is no demonstrated request-size ceiling** (corrected 2026-09-11 — measured, single-call MCP writes of 27,415 / 29,824 / 44,387-character bodies all succeeded from AR_orc; see the MCP-TRANSPORT REJECTION bullet in the retry ladder for the full measurement and for the two alerts it overturns). `ops.sp_amend_run_note` (`bigquery/170_run_log_note_write_time_guard.sql`) FILLS A BLANK note on a terminal row left by a run that is still in progress — and ONLY that: it refuses any row whose note is already non-blank, any non-terminal row, and any row logged more than 6 hours ago (`REFUSED: no eligible row … it does not rewrite history`), so there is deliberately NO append/extend path for a note already written — put late detail in an `events.decision_log` entry or the next run's note instead (corrected 2026-10-11, W5 SPEC-DEFECT NOTICE INTAKE, closing D2a's `amend_run_note_spec_says_extend_impl_says_fill`, alert `ca5a05e2-eca2-4d93-aabe-a158d4062609`; the procedure is right and is NOT loosened, the earlier "extending" wording was wrong). It is not a workaround for this error and trimming is never the answer to it.

## H-PRE-17 — THE ops.web_calls BATCH INSERT BELONGS HERE — immediately before the terminal call above (pinned 20260819). T

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 282) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

  **THE `ops.web_calls` BATCH INSERT BELONGS HERE — immediately before the terminal call above (pinned 2026-08-19).** The shared **"Metered external calls"** rule's part (d) obliges every routine that touched Tavily / `web_search` / `web_fetch` / FMP / HF to append one `ops.web_calls` row per call, batched into a SINGLE `INSERT` near session end (`bigquery/174_web_call_telemetry.sql`). That obligation was stated ONLY in the shared-rules section ~450 lines below this template, and it did not survive contact with the fleet — **the identical failure shape the `<note>` rule above exists to close** ("the text a session actually re-read at logging time was silent about it"). Measured live 2026-08-19: `ops.web_calls` held 54 rows, **all of them stamped `run_date = 2026-08-17`** — the day the table was created — and only from **D1 and W3**; every other routine has logged nothing, ever. D1's own **2026-08-18** run then fetched a published breadth figure (it wrote `EQUITY_BREADTH_PCT` 68.19 for that date, which its own step spec sources via a Tavily/extract fetch) and cited two external URLs in `Daily.md`, and logged **zero** `ops.web_calls` rows. So the telemetry is not a table that was never applied — it was applied 2026-08-17 15:13 UTC and is live and correct; what stopped was ROUTINES WRITING TO IT, on the first day after the two sessions that wrote the rule. Write the `INSERT` here, inside the same best-effort wrapper, as part of finishing the run — never as a separate errand to remember at some unspecified "session end".
  **A run that made no metered call correctly writes no row** — but from the table alone that is INDISTINGUISHABLE from a run that made calls and forgot, which is precisely why `state.web_spend_month.has_unreported_runs` exists, and why W5's EXTERNAL-SPEND DIGEST must NAME the routines it is TRUE for instead of quoting a fleet total that reads as complete. Per the **State provenance** rule, a spend figure drawn from this table while that flag is TRUE is a **FLOOR, not a total**, and must be said to be one. **SO AFFIRM A ZERO IN `<note>` — ONE BLANKET LINE, NOT A PER-SURFACE DENIAL (added 2026-08-30, closes the `telemetry_zero_affirmation_scope` info alert).** On any run that writes NO `ops.web_calls` row, the terminal `<note>` must carry a SINGLE line affirming the zero across EVERY metered surface at once — Tavily, `web_search`/`web_fetch`, FMP and HF, orchestrator AND sub-agents — in W3's 2026-08-23 shape: `METERED SPEND: ZERO. No Tavily, web_search, web_fetch, FMP or HF call by the orchestrator or ANY sub-agent — every fact came from BigQuery, the IBKR connector, or repo files, all free. Consequently no ops.web_calls rows are owed or written — an honest zero, not an omission.` A note that denies only the surfaces a routine's OWN text happens to name — W5's 2026-08-24 `market-calendar auto-extend NOT triggered ... so no FMP call spent`, plus a separate `0 new/closing events.nogo_shadow rows`, is the measured instance — forces OPS0's STEP 5 **CONDITIONAL-SURFACE CARVE-OUT** to INFER that those are the routine's only metered surfaces, which is the writer-side form of the enumerate-by-routine-id defect that carve-out was widened away from on 2026-08-26. Per STEP 5's own rule an absent or ambiguous affirmation is NOT suppressed but RAISED, and because its resolve clause can only fire on a row-BEARING run, a correctly-quiet routine then carries a false `web_call_coverage_gap` warning it cannot clear until it next has a metered cycle. This binds EVERY `expected_metered` routine — D1, D2a, W1, W2, W3, W5, M1a, M2, M3, Q2, Q3, SL1, OPS1, OPS2 per `bigquery/200_web_call_coverage_obligation_floor.sql` — not only the ones whose metered surfaces are conditional, and it is stated HERE rather than per-routine for the same reason the batch-INSERT pinning above is: this is the text a session re-reads when it finishes a run.

## H-PRE-18 — CATCHUP EVIDENCE WINDOW (owner directive 20260725) — every routine, right after run logging, BESTEFFORT, neve

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 285) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

- **CATCH-UP EVIDENCE WINDOW (owner directive 2026-07-25) — every routine, right after run logging, BEST-EFFORT, never gates.** Principle: this run's evidence/analysis reach is **(this routine's own last successful completion, now]**, not a fixed lookback — resolve it with `SELECT window_start_ts, never_completed, window_days FROM state.routine_catchup_window WHERE routine='<ID>'` (`bigquery/105_routine_catchup_window.sql`, one row per routine, keyed off the same `ops.run_log` completions the Run-logging bullet above just established; `window_start_ts` = the timestamp of this routine's own last `completed` row, or, if `never_completed`, a cadence-sized fallback keyed to its `ops/cadence.yaml` monitor class). Wherever a routine's own section below says "today" / "since the last run" / "the prior week/month/quarter" for **evidence gathering** (Daily.md scan ranges, `events.decision_log` lookbacks, file diffs, etc.), `window_start_ts` is the actual lower bound whenever it reaches further back than that phrase's normal cadence — it only ever widens the phrase, never narrows it.
  - **WHY (the aging-alert trap).** `ops.sp_auto_resolve_alerts` Rule 2 (`bigquery/34_alert_lifecycle.sql`, invoked by the Alert auto-resolve bullet above) clears a `missed_run` alert once the alerted day is simply >1 day stale — with ZERO completion evidence required. A cleared alert is therefore never proof a missed day's evidence was actually analyzed; the 2026-07-23/24 connector outage showed exactly this — missed days went uncovered because each routine's next run only ever looked at "today" or its own fixed lookback once the alert had aged off. This protocol is the actual coverage mechanism the aging-out resolver was silently assumed to be.
  - **DECISION SCOPING (unchanged).** Only the EVIDENCE window widens — decisions and actions stay current-session-scoped exactly as **Decision discipline** above already requires: no retroactive orders, no backdated staging/period writes, nothing dated into a missed period. A wider window means this run *sees* more history; it does not let this run *act* as if it were an earlier date.
  - **MULTI-PERIOD OUTPUT RULE.** File first-line period markers (see "File-write conventions for routine outputs") stay CURRENT-period always — never stamp a file with a missed period's marker. If `window_start_ts` spans more than one period for that routine's cadence, cover the missed periods as labeled sub-sections in the body, **oldest first**, ahead of the current-period content.
  - **WRITE-ONCE RULE.** Catch-up coverage reuses each routine's EXISTING idempotency keys (`events.decision_log` entry conventions, `ops.catchup_refire_log` miss_keys, per-finding dedup, etc.) — one BigQuery write per distinct finding even when the session's widened window covers several missed periods at once; never re-emit a write for a period/finding already durably recorded.
  - **TELEMETRY.** When `window_days` materially exceeds this routine's own cadence (**>1.5x** its normal lookback — daily >1.5 days, weekly >10.5 days, etc.), append the machine-parseable token **`CATCHUP[window_days=<N>]`** to the run's completion `<note>`, mirroring the `RETRY[...]`/`DEPWAIT[...]` token convention above. Routine cadence-normal windows (`window_days` at or near the fallback) need no token.
  - **NON-GATING + FALLBACK.** Best-effort only, same posture as Alert auto-resolve above and the OPS0 "must NEVER be blocked" rule: if `state.routine_catchup_window` is unreadable (BigQuery hiccup, view missing) or the query errors, fall back to this routine's own existing cadence-default lookback, proceed, and note the fallback in `<note>` (e.g. `catchup view unreadable, used cadence-default lookback`) — never halt or degrade the run on this view's absence.
  - **Reference implementations generalized here — cite, don't reinvent per routine:** D1's SCAN WINDOW (marker + git-commit-timestamp + fixed-lookback fallback), D3's golden-scenario check (`git log --since=<D3's own last completed ops.run_log run>`), W5's "since the last W5 run" bullets, and the queue-drain routines (SL2, SL5, SL1, AR_att, AR_orc) all independently solved this same "how far back since I last ran" problem in routine-specific ways; this bullet is the single mechanism the rest of the plan should point back to instead of restating it. **WHERE THE WINDOW IS READ FROM GIT, RUN THE HISTORY-DEPTH PRECHECK FIRST (§Execution environment; added 2026-09-08 with that rule).** This bullet's whole job is to make a window WIDER than the routine's normal cadence, and the wider it gets the likelier it reaches past the routine container's shallow-clone boundary (measured ~7 days back on both occasions it was checked). Every git-sourced window above is affected — D1's git-commit-timestamp leg, D3's golden-scenario `git log --since`, D2's `Daily.md` catch-up read — and on a truncated history each returns "nothing changed in that window" in exactly the shape of a genuinely quiet period. That silently NARROWS the very window this bullet exists to widen, which is the one failure this mechanism must not have. The precheck's step 1 is a local, no-network call and the SessionStart hook has usually already deepened the clone, so this costs nothing on a healthy fire; on an UNDEEPENABLE one, fall back to the non-git leg (`state.routine_catchup_window`, which is BigQuery-sourced and depth-immune) and say in `<note>` that the git leg was unavailable. **Distinct from two similarly-named windows — do not conflate:** the **DEPENDENCY-WAIT WINDOW** (Dependency-gate bullet below) is this routine waiting on an UPSTREAM routine to complete before it may run at all; this CATCH-UP EVIDENCE WINDOW is this routine widening how far back its OWN evidence reads once it does run. Likewise distinct from **`catchup_safe`** (`ops/cadence.yaml`), which governs whether OPS0 may auto-refire a routine's missed TRIGGER — a live-ops scheduling concept, not an evidence-reach concept; a routine can be `catchup_safe: false` (e.g. the queue-driven ids) and still fully benefit from this bullet. **Blinding is unchanged**: this bullet governs only how far back in TIME a routine's evidence reaches, never WHICH sources it may read — AR_att's strict blinding and M1a's file-boundary blinding apply exactly as before regardless of window width.

## H-PRE-19 — DEPENDENCYWAIT WINDOW (owner directive 20260718; v2 same day, adversarially reviewed) — an unsatisfied depend

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 300) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

  **DEPENDENCY-WAIT WINDOW (owner directive 2026-07-18; v2 same day, adversarially reviewed) — an unsatisfied dependency is a WAIT driven by EVIDENCE, not an instant halt and not a blind hour.** If `sp_assert_deps` still aborts after its mechanical tolerances (the §38 marker backfill above, the midnight-crossing grace below) and the git-evidence fallback found nothing, do not halt. Log `'started'` NOW via the best-effort `sp_routine_start` template (the wait must be visible in `ops.run_log`), then poll ~every 10 min; each poll = re-`CALL ops.sp_assert_deps(...)` (re-runs the §38 backfill, so an upstream landing mid-window self-heals) + ONE cheap read of the upstream's `ops.run_log` rows for today + the same-day double-run-guard re-check on YOURSELF (if another session completed — or per the in-progress variant, is actively running — THIS routine today, output the guard's standard line and END IMMEDIATELY). Decide from the upstream's actual state:
  - **Gate PASSES** → proceed with the run; best-effort `CALL ops.sp_auto_resolve_alerts()` right there — the gate raised its `missing_dependency` alert on the FIRST failed call (it alerts before it RAISEs), and nothing else clears it mid-loop; this call resolves it now instead of letting a stale critical linger for hours.
  - **Upstream `'started'` within the last ~2h, no terminal row → ALIVE:** keep polling; the total window may extend past 60 min to a hard ceiling of **120 min** (still far under `state.stalled_runs`' 6h fast tier).
  - **Upstream terminal `'failed'`/`'halted'` today:** REFIRE once if refireable (below); otherwise **FUTILE — exhaust immediately**, do not sit out the remaining polls (the gate's alert is already durable; the `<note>` records the upstream's terminal status). **A same-day HALT-RECORD commit for the upstream on `origin/main` (RUNBOOK §48 subject convention; file `ops/spikes/*-halt-record.md` naming the upstream) establishes this same condition even with NO `ops.run_log` row (added 2026-10-11, alert `d8da6e78-f4f3-4549-8389-91d3be3c3551`, half B):** a connector-outage halt can never write the row, because the run log and the alert sink are the connector that was down, so the most certainly futile halt class would otherwise read as "no upstream row" and buy the full base window. Take FUTILE, exhaust immediately, cite the commit in the `<note>`, and never convert it into a `completed` row (the REFIRE branch is still taken first where the upstream is refireable).
  - **NO upstream row today** and its scheduled slot (`ops/cadence.yaml` `time_local`) passed ≥45 min ago: **ACTIVE REPAIR** once per run if refireable; otherwise plain 60-min window. *(Clarified 2026-10-11, alert `d8da6e78-f4f3-4549-8389-91d3be3c3551`, half C: this test is re-evaluated at EVERY poll, not only at fire time, so a downstream that fires less than 45 min behind its upstream's slot — AR_orc 18:35 behind AR_att 18:00, 35 min — reaches it after the first poll or two; and for a non-refireable upstream (AR_att is `catchup_safe: false`) the branch resolves to the plain window regardless, so the 35-min spacing changes nothing. Where such an upstream's own intake is empty the wait is futile on the merits — see AR_orc's UPSTREAM-HALT LIMB.)*
  - **Budgets and clamps:** 60 min base / 120 min alive-or-refired ceiling; SEPARATE budget from the transient ladder's ~30-min cap (a run may legitimately spend both); entered at most ONCE per run (all declared deps together); **never poll past 23:30 America/Denver** (shared clamp — see the ladder bullet); and if the same missing upstream chain is ALREADY covered by an open `missing_dependency` alert raised by an earlier routine today, HALVE the window to ~30 min — the chain is already alarmed, don't serialize full windows down a D1→D2→D3 chain. Does NOT apply to the trading-enable gate or any order-guard block — those remain immediate, correct halts.

## H-PRE-20 — SAMEDAY DOUBLERUN GUARD, generalized (completenesscritic finding N4, 20260716) — binds every catchup_safe: tr

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 313) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

- **SAME-DAY DOUBLE-RUN GUARD, generalized (completeness-critic finding N-4, 2026-07-16) — binds every `catchup_safe: true` routine in `ops/cadence.yaml`, including W4's queue-only research handoff, PLUS D2 and D2a (rescoped 2026-08-09).** D2 and D2a are both `catchup_safe: false` yet each independently exposed to same-day double-invocation — D2 via D2a's own chain-call, which fires regardless of D2's catch-up eligibility; D2a via a manual re-run or a retry after a partial failure. Neither exposure is gated by the OPS0/OPS2 catch-up exclusions: that set (D2, D2a, M4, Q4, A3, SL4 — see the REFIREABLE definition above) only stops OPS0/OPS2 from double-REFIRING a routine themselves, not a same-day re-run or chain-call arriving from another source. The queue-driven AR_att/AR_orc/SL2/SL5/**M1R** remain outside this rule (M1R added to this exemption 2026-09-06, W5 SPEC-DEFECT NOTICE INTAKE, closing M1R's `double_run_guard_scope_omits_m1r`: M1R is a fifth queue-driven `catchup_safe: true` routine, registered 2026-09-05 — after this bullet last rescoped on 2026-08-09 — so the leading clause formally swept it in while the exemption sentence had not caught up. Nothing was ever at risk: M1R STEP 1 states its queue gate IS its double-run guard, and a second same-day fire re-reads that gate, finds the item complete or absent, and no-ops. The cost was prose-only — a session reading this bullet alone could have bolted on a redundant guard M1R says it does not need, or read the omission as evidence M1R is unguarded); W4 is inside because a late original trigger can otherwise duplicate its recovered queue writes. OPS0 may catch up and refire a routine that missed its scheduled trigger; its own `ops.catchup_refire_log` only stops OPS0 from refiring the SAME routine twice from OPS0's own side — it does nothing to stop a late-firing ORIGINAL platform trigger from independently double-running a routine OPS0 already refired today. **Every routine below this bullet's scope carries a one-line pointer back to this text (in its own Observability preamble, not restated in full) reading: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM ops.run_log WHERE routine='<ID>' AND run_date=<today, America/Denver> AND status='completed'`; if `>= 1`, output "`<ID>` already completed today (a prior fire — OPS0 catch-up or the routine's own scheduled trigger — already completed; this is the expected redundant re-invocation, not an error)." and END IMMEDIATELY.** **ONE-TIME OWNER WAIVER (added 2026-10-20, owner directive in an interactive session; `bigquery/260_guard_waivers.sql`) — binds every routine this bullet binds, and every per-routine pointer below inherits it without restating it.** Before ending on this guard, read `SELECT waiver_id, reason FROM state.guard_waivers_open WHERE routine='<ID>' AND guard='same_day_double_run'`. No row: end as above. A row: CONSUME it first — `INSERT INTO ops.guard_waivers (waiver_id, routine, guard, run_date, event, actor, note) VALUES ('<waiver_id>', '<ID>', 'same_day_double_run', <today, America/Denver>, 'CONSUMED', '<ID>:<this session id>', 'consumed by <ID> fire <UTC timestamp>')` (the six NOT NULL, no-default columns are exactly those; `event_id` and `event_ts` default) — then PROCEED as a normal run: `sp_routine_start` and every gate below (arsenal kill-switch, dependency, trading-enable) apply unchanged, and the run `<note>` states `double-run guard waived by <waiver_id>`. A waiver is GRANTED only by the owner (an interactive session, `actor` `owner:<session>`) for ONE routine, ONE guard and ONE Denver `run_date`; it is single-use (the CONSUMED row closes it, so a third same-day fire ends on the guard), it expires with its `run_date` (`state.guard_waivers_open` filters on `state.trading_day_today`), and it waives nothing else. A scheduled routine never grants one. The audit trail is the table itself. The evening-slot daily cohort uses the documented noon-threshold cycle-aware variant. In addition to the completed-count, if another session's `'started'` row for `<ID>`/today exists with `log_ts` within the last **3 hours** and no terminal row yet, treat it as an in-flight original and END IMMEDIATELY. A `'started'` row OLDER than 3h with no terminal row is treated as a dead session and does NOT suppress this run. Run this check FIRST, before run logging, the dependency gate, or any routine work.
  - **QUIESCENCE REBUTTAL to the 3h in-flight rule (added 2026-08-18, SL3 — closes the evening-slot unrecoverable-day defect).** The 3-hour figure is a **liveness PROXY**, chosen because a successor session normally has no direct way to tell a predecessor that is still working from one that died mid-run; it is the same constant as the "repo-wide dead-run criterion" the dust-order mutex cites. Where a **direct** liveness signal exists it governs, and the proxy yields to it. Every routine in this plan is BigQuery-bodied, so a session that is genuinely working leaves query jobs continuously: read `` `stock-trading-498512.region-us`.INFORMATION_SCHEMA.JOBS_BY_PROJECT `` for jobs created since the predecessor's `'started'` `log_ts`, and if the newest job attributable to that routine's own work is **older than 45 minutes**, the predecessor is DEAD — PROCEED with this run, and record the token **`DEADPRIOR[prior_started=<HH:MM MT>;last_job=<HH:MM MT>;quiet_min=<N>]`** in the completion `<note>` (same token grammar as `RETRY[...]`/`CATCHUP[...]`). 45 min is deliberately far above the quantity it actually bounds — the GAP between consecutive BigQuery jobs inside a live run — and far below the 3h proxy, so the rebuttal can only ever fire on a session that has demonstrably stopped doing anything. **RE-MEASURED 2026-09-02 (SL3 diligence sweep, measured against `JOBS_BY_PROJECT` rather than inferred): the figure that stood here was stale AND the wrong quantity, and the second half is the one that matters.** It read "SL3's last twelve runs took **4-9 minutes** start-to-finish". **Total runtime neither bounds nor is bounded by inter-job silence** — a 19-minute run can hold a 7-minute quiet gap, and the 131-minute 2026-08-18 recovery window (this rule's own worked instance) held a 23-minute one — so a session that compares the 45-min bar against a RUNTIME figure is checking a number that cannot answer the question, however accurate that number is. It was not accurate either: SL3's last twelve completed runs span **3-19 minutes** for ordinary fires, plus 131 and 225 on the 2026-08-18 / 08-20 incident nights — wrong at BOTH ends of the stated 4-9. **The right measurement, over the thirteen SL3 run windows 2026-08-16..2026-09-01: the max gap between consecutive jobs inside a window is 1-7 minutes on every ordinary run, and 23 min / 16 min on the two incident windows.** So the bar clears the worst gap ever observed by ~2x and an ordinary run's by ~6x — the conclusion above is unchanged and now rests on the quantity it is actually about. Note also that the two incident windows carry **390** and **680** jobs against 15-61 for an ordinary run: that is direct corroboration of the project-wide caution in the next sentence, not evidence of SL3's own job rate. **Absence of a pushed branch or of an `ops.routine_commit_markers` row is NOT evidence of death** — a legitimately-running session has neither until its final step; use them only to CORROBORATE a quiescence finding, never to establish one. The job-history read is project-wide, so attribute by query shape and timing, not by assuming the project is otherwise idle (the `alert_emailer` poll, scheduled queries and CI writes all appear there).
  - **WHY the rebuttal is needed — the proxy is worst exactly where its cost is highest.** For the EVENING-slot daily cohort (D1/D2a/D2/SL3, ~20:00 MT) a 3-hour shadow cast from a 20:06 death runs to 23:06 MT, against a session-wide rule that forbids waiting past **23:30 America/Denver** and a `run_date` pinned at session start — so a predecessor dying in the first minutes of its slot costs the WHOLE day: the successor no-ops silently, `state.cadence_watch` books a missed run, that routine's downstream heartbeat assertions fire (for SL3, W5's `meta_monitoring_heartbeat` → `arsenal_heartbeat_missing`), and `state.stalled_runs` only raises `routine_stalled` at its 6h mark, on a session that had been dead for five of them. **Not hypothetical — SL3 hit this exact shape on 2026-08-18:** a fire logged `'started'` at 20:06 MT, issued its last BigQuery job at 20:52 MT, and never wrote a terminal row; the 22:07 MT re-fire sat 2h01m inside the shadow and would have been suppressed by the letter of the rule. It proceeded on this rebuttal (75 min of quiescence, no `claude/sl3-2026-08-18` on `origin`, no commit marker) and the day was recovered. Note the interaction that makes this class SELF-CONCEALING, and why the token above is mandatory rather than decorative: the successor's own `'completed'` row closes the orphaned `'started'` for `state.stalled_runs`, which anti-joins terminal rows on `(routine, run_date)` — so a recovered day leaves NO stalled-run alert and no other durable trace that a session died at all.
  - **VISIBILITY — a suppressed run must not be silent.** A session that DOES end on the in-flight rule currently writes nothing whatsoever: correctly no `ops.run_log` row (a suppressed run must never satisfy `cadence_watch`/`stalled_runs` for work nobody did), but also no queryable record that a successor ever arrived — so if the "in-flight original" is itself dead, the day is lost with no evidence anyone tried. Before ending on this rule, write ONE `events.decision_log` row via `CALL ops.sp_log_decision(<today>,'ops-note',...)` naming the predecessor's `'started'` `log_ts`, this session's own timestamp, and the quiescence figure that failed to clear the 45-minute bar. Use `sp_log_decision`, **NOT** a new `ops.alerts` category: an unregistered category latches by default under `ops.alert_policy`'s fail-closed allowlist and would demand a human resolve for what is usually a correct and healthy suppression — precisely the human gate the SISA posture removes.

### Pass 2 input — q0089, 2026-10-30

_The newly removed second-pass text is retained in this verbatim input; history only._

- **SAME-DAY DOUBLE-RUN GUARD, generalized (completeness-critic finding N-4, 2026-07-16) — binds every `catchup_safe: true` routine in `ops/cadence.yaml`, including W4's queue-only research handoff, PLUS D2 and D2a (rescoped 2026-08-09).** Queue-driven AR_att/AR_orc/SL2/SL5/**M1R** are exempt: their queue gates prevent duplicates; W4 is included because a late original trigger can duplicate recovered queue writes. OPS0's `ops.catchup_refire_log` prevents its own duplicate refires only; it does not guard a late original trigger. **Every routine below this bullet's scope carries a one-line pointer back to this text (in its own Observability preamble, not restated in full) reading: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM ops.run_log WHERE routine='<ID>' AND run_date=<today, America/Denver> AND status='completed'`; if `>= 1`, output "`<ID>` already completed today (a prior fire — OPS0 catch-up or the routine's own scheduled trigger — already completed; this is the expected redundant re-invocation, not an error)." and END IMMEDIATELY.** **ONE-TIME OWNER WAIVER (added 2026-10-20, owner directive in an interactive session; `bigquery/260_guard_waivers.sql`) — binds every routine this bullet binds, and every per-routine pointer below inherits it without restating it.** Before ending on this guard, read `SELECT waiver_id, reason FROM state.guard_waivers_open WHERE routine='<ID>' AND guard='same_day_double_run'`. No row: end as above. A row: CONSUME it first — `INSERT INTO ops.guard_waivers (waiver_id, routine, guard, run_date, event, actor, note) VALUES ('<waiver_id>', '<ID>', 'same_day_double_run', <today, America/Denver>, 'CONSUMED', '<ID>:<this session id>', 'consumed by <ID> fire <UTC timestamp>')` (the six NOT NULL, no-default columns are exactly those; `event_id` and `event_ts` default) — then PROCEED as a normal run: `sp_routine_start` and every gate below (arsenal kill-switch, dependency, trading-enable) apply unchanged, and the run `<note>` states `double-run guard waived by <waiver_id>`. A waiver is GRANTED only by the owner (an interactive session, `actor` `owner:<session>`) for ONE routine, ONE guard and ONE Denver `run_date`; it is single-use (the CONSUMED row closes it, so a third same-day fire ends on the guard), it expires with its `run_date` (`state.guard_waivers_open` filters on `state.trading_day_today`), and it waives nothing else. A scheduled routine never grants one. The audit trail is the table itself. The evening-slot daily cohort uses the documented noon-threshold cycle-aware variant. In addition to the completed-count, if another session's `'started'` row for `<ID>`/today exists with `log_ts` within the last **3 hours** and no terminal row yet, treat it as an in-flight original and END IMMEDIATELY. A `'started'` row OLDER than 3h with no terminal row is treated as a dead session and does NOT suppress this run. Run this check FIRST, before run logging, the dependency gate, or any routine work.
  - **QUIESCENCE REBUTTAL to the 3h in-flight rule (added 2026-08-18, SL3 — closes the evening-slot unrecoverable-day defect).** The 3-hour figure is a **liveness PROXY**, chosen because a successor session normally has no direct way to tell a predecessor that is still working from one that died mid-run; it is the same constant as the "repo-wide dead-run criterion" the dust-order mutex cites. Where a **direct** liveness signal exists it governs, and the proxy yields to it. Every routine in this plan is BigQuery-bodied, so a session that is genuinely working leaves query jobs continuously: read `` `stock-trading-498512.region-us`.INFORMATION_SCHEMA.JOBS_BY_PROJECT `` for jobs created since the predecessor's `'started'` `log_ts`, and if the newest job attributable to that routine's own work is **older than 45 minutes**, the predecessor is DEAD — PROCEED with this run, and record the token **`DEADPRIOR[prior_started=<HH:MM MT>;last_job=<HH:MM MT>;quiet_min=<N>]`** in the completion `<note>` (same token grammar as `RETRY[...]`/`CATCHUP[...]`). 45 min is deliberately far above the quantity it actually bounds — the GAP between consecutive BigQuery jobs inside a live run — and far below the 3h proxy, so the rebuttal can only ever fire on a session that has demonstrably stopped doing anything. Compare 45 minutes against inter-job silence, never total runtime, which cannot establish quiescence. **Absence of a pushed branch or of an `ops.routine_commit_markers` row is NOT evidence of death** — a legitimately-running session has neither until its final step; use them only to CORROBORATE a quiescence finding, never to establish one. The job-history read is project-wide, so attribute by query shape and timing, not by assuming the project is otherwise idle (the `alert_emailer` poll, scheduled queries and CI writes all appear there).
  - **WHY the rebuttal is needed — the proxy is worst exactly where its cost is highest.** An evening-slot predecessor can die early enough that the 3h proxy shadows the whole remaining operational day. The successor's `completed` row closes the orphaned `started` for `state.stalled_runs`, which anti-joins terminal rows on `(routine, run_date)`, so the `DEADPRIOR[...]` token is mandatory evidence of recovery.
  - **VISIBILITY — a suppressed run must not be silent.** A session that DOES end on the in-flight rule currently writes nothing whatsoever: correctly no `ops.run_log` row (a suppressed run must never satisfy `cadence_watch`/`stalled_runs` for work nobody did), but also no queryable record that a successor ever arrived — so if the "in-flight original" is itself dead, the day is lost with no evidence anyone tried. Before ending on this rule, write ONE `events.decision_log` row via `CALL ops.sp_log_decision(<today>,'ops-note',...)` naming the predecessor's `'started'` `log_ts`, this session's own timestamp, and the quiescence figure that failed to clear the 45-minute bar. Use `sp_log_decision`, **NOT** a new `ops.alerts` category: an unregistered category latches by default under `ops.alert_policy`'s fail-closed allowlist and would demand a human resolve for what is usually a correct and healthy suppression — precisely the human gate the SISA posture removes. *(Pre-2026-10-30 history and evidence for this rule: `ops/plan_history/00_preamble.md` § H-PRE-20.)*

## H-PRE-21 — RUNLOG GAP INTERPRETATION — a zerorow day is NOT an outage until state.fleet_blackout_days says so (added 202

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 318) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

- **RUN-LOG GAP INTERPRETATION — a zero-row day is NOT an outage until `state.fleet_blackout_days` says so (added 2026-08-23, W1 `cadence_outage` false positive `f6ff58ee`).** Before raising ANY alert, note, or decision-log entry asserting that routines failed to run, you MUST resolve the claim against `bigquery/196_cadence_expected_history.sql`. **Use the object that matches the SHAPE of your claim — they are not interchangeable:** for a FLEET-WIDE claim, `SELECT * FROM state.fleet_blackout_days` — zero rows means no day in the trailing 90 had every expected routine silent, so a date absent from it is an EXPECTED non-run day, not a missed one. For a SINGLE-ROUTINE claim, `SELECT * FROM state.cadence_expected_history WHERE routine = '<ID>' AND run_date = <date>` and read `expected`, `in_service` and `rows_logged` — a routine is missing only if `expected AND in_service AND rows_logged = 0`. **`state.fleet_blackout_days` cannot answer a single-routine question and must not be used for one:** it aggregates per DATE, carries no `routine` column at all, and only fires when the ENTIRE expected cohort was silent — so on a day when one routine died and its siblings ran normally it correctly returns nothing, and reading that as "no routine was missed" inverts the answer. Do not substitute a raw `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM ops.run_log WHERE run_date = ...`: a raw row count cannot tell an expected-idle day apart from a real outage, and since the 2026-08-08 daily-tier consolidation the fleet legitimately logs **ZERO rows every Friday and every Saturday** (`monitor_class: daily_sun_thu` = `dow NOT IN (6,7)`, `ops/cadence.yaml` header, the `daily_sun_thu` monitor-class definition). That is precisely what W1 misread on 2026-08-23 — it counted zero rows on Fri 2026-08-21 and Sat 2026-08-22 and raised a WARNING claiming a "FLEET-WIDE SCHEDULED-TRIGGER OUTAGE"; W3 refuted it the same day, but the operator email had already gone out. `state.cadence_watch` and `ops.sp_sq_cadence_check` were correct and silent throughout — they encode the day-of-week predicate and had nothing to miss.
  **Why the existing monitors did not save W1, so this is not filed as a missing predicate:** every cadence object built on `state.trading_day_today` answers only for **today** (it is `CURRENT_DATE('America/Denver')`), so a session looking BACKWARD at elapsed days had no cadence-aware object it could query at all and improvised one. `state.cadence_expected_history` is the retrospective form that closes that hole; it applies the identical `monitor_class` predicates per past date. Per the `sp_log_decision`-not-a-new-category rule above, if you still believe a gap is real after checking, record it with `CALL ops.sp_log_decision(...)` — do NOT invent an `ops.alerts` category for it. `cadence_outage` was unregistered in `ops.alert_policy` and therefore LATCHED under the fail-closed default, requiring a human resolve for a non-event. Genuine missed runs already have an owner and a registered category: `missed_run`, raised by `ops.sp_sq_cadence_check` off `state.cadence_watch`.
- **Upstream-output FRESHNESS check (W4 research handoff plus M4 / Q4 / A3 action-conversion routines) — REWRITTEN 2026-08-10, primary signal changed from marker-equality to the period-aware `ops.run_log` read `sp_assert_deps` already performs; origin: W4's own 2026-08-09 self-referral.** W4's 2026-08-09 cycle (`events.decision_log`, `entry_type='action-conversion'`, title "W4 2026-W32 (Sun 2026-08-09) cycle summary — sections A through F") found and logged, but deliberately did not alert on, a defect in the marker-equality design this bullet used to specify — closing with "FINDING REFERRED TO W5 — PERIOD-MARKER ALIASING WEAKENS THE W4/M4/Q4/A3 FRESHNESS GATE." W5's own 2026-08-09 cycle ran the same day (`entry_type='routine-outcome'`, title "W5 2026-W33 outcome"), covered seven other findings in detail (the NO-GO counterfactual repair, `entry_type` vocabulary drift, wash-sale, calibration, arsenal, park, research-screen), and never mentions the referral — the call went unactioned, not merely undecided pending a later cycle. This revision is that action, plus an independent correction to this bullet's own stale premise (below).

## H-PRE-22 — PRIMARY signal, for every upstream this routine already declares as a dependency: the SAME CALL ops.sp_assert

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 322) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

  **PRIMARY signal, for every upstream this routine already declares as a dependency: the SAME `CALL ops.sp_assert_deps('<ID>', <deps>, <today>)` the Dependency gate bullet above already requires before `sp_routine_start` — no new call, no new arithmetic.** `sp_assert_deps` (`bigquery/114_period_aware_dependency_gate.sql`) satisfies a period-class dependency by ANY `completed` `ops.run_log` row inside the DOWNSTREAM's own current period: for `weekly_sun` (W1/W2/W3) that period is `DATE_SUB(in_run_date, INTERVAL (EXTRACT(DAYOFWEEK FROM in_run_date) - 1) DAY)` through `in_run_date` — the Sunday of the run week through today (`bigquery/114`'s `dep_win` CTE, `weekly_sun` arm; `ops/cadence.yaml`'s `id: W1` entry comment on W1's trigger retiming states the same bucketing rule: "bigquery/114 buckets weekly_sun by the Sunday of the run week") — and for `monthly_ftd` / `quarterly_ftd` / `annual_ftd` (M1b/M2/M3, Q2/Q3, A1/A2) it is `DATE_TRUNC(in_run_date, MONTH/QUARTER/YEAR)` through today (`dep_win`'s `monthly_ftd`/`quarterly_ftd`/`annual_ftd` arms). This is already computed and CI-verified inside the one call these routines make for the Dependency gate above; it is period-cycle-correct in a way a bare marker-string comparison is not (see WHY, next). **The premise that made the marker primary — "`sp_assert_deps` ... is INERT for the research feeders that have not yet adopted run-logging (only D1/D2/D3/AR are monitored)" — is STALE AND FALSE as of 2026-08-10.** `ops.run_log` shows `completed` rows for W1/W2/W3 continuously since 2026-06-21 (BEFORE this bullet was even added, 2026-06-24), for M1b/M2/M3 and Q2/Q3 since 2026-07-01, and for A1/A2 since 2026-07-28 (first row a 02:51 MT post-midnight stamp for a trigger that fired the evening of 2026-07-27 — the same midnight-crossing pattern the Midnight-crossing grace paragraph below documents for other routines). `bigquery/114`'s `ever` CTE already self-bootstraps monitoring for exactly this case — an upstream absent from `ops.run_log` is treated as SATISFIED, never as a block — so declaring these six dependencies was always safe, and each became a REAL gate, not merely a safe no-op, the moment its own upstream started logging (i.e. before this bullet was even written for four of the six), unnoticed until now.

## H-PRE-23 — A corroborating marker/shape mismatch found DESPITE sp_assert_deps passing is deliberately NOT folded into th

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 332) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

  **A corroborating marker/shape mismatch found DESPITE `sp_assert_deps` passing is deliberately NOT folded into the missing-dependency abort above (corrected 2026-08-10, same-day adversarial review of this rewrite — the paragraph above previously listed the marker mismatch as a third abort trigger, directly contradicting the WHY-THE-MARKER-CANNOT-BE-THE-PROOF paragraph above that (three paragraphs up from here) which already calls such a mismatch "not proof-by-itself of a missing dependency").** Two independent reasons it must stay non-fatal: **(1) for the WEEKLY case (W4), folding it in would re-manufacture the exact Mon/Sun aliasing this rewrite exists to close, just on the opposite failure edge.** `W1`/`W2`/`W3` markers are ISO-week (`YYYY-Www`, Monday-anchored — `scripts/check_cadence_marker.py`'s `weekly` marker regex and `expected_marker()`), but the `weekly_sun` period `sp_assert_deps` actually gates on is Sunday-anchored (`dep_win`'s `weekly_sun` arm above) — so whenever W4 legitimately runs even one calendar day later than its upstreams, an ISO-week boundary is crossed and the "current `YYYY-WW`" W4 expects (computed from ITS OWN `<today>`, per the retrospective-offset table above) will not equal the marker a same-period upstream correctly wrote a day earlier, even though the content is fully fresh. **This is not a rare corner case — it is the flagship scenario `bigquery/114`'s period-aware design exists to accept:** the Dependency gate bullet above states directly that "W4's W1/W2/W3 deps are satisfied by a Sunday completion even when W4 itself runs Monday afternoon," and W4 has already done exactly this for real (`ops.run_log`: `routine='W4'`, `run_date='2026-08-03'` [a Monday], `status='completed'`, catching up the missed W31 cycle) — a live-fleet pattern, not a hypothetical. Aborting the very case the PRIMARY signal was rewritten to correctly ADMIT would make this rewrite net-negative for the weekly cohort: trading a false PASS for a false HALT on the routine's own designed-for late-running path. (M4/Q4/A3 do not carry this specific exposure — their monthly/quarterly/annual markers are calendar-truncated the same way `monthly_ftd`/`quarterly_ftd`/`annual_ftd` are, `scripts/check_cadence_marker.py` `expected_marker()`'s monthly/quarterly/annual branches, so no Mon/Sun-style anchor mismatch exists for them; this fix is written into the shared bullet because the bullet is shared, but the exposure it closes is W4-only.) **(2) the M1b mis-stamp this section cites as the marker check's own motivating incident is itself evidence that a mismatch does not mean stale content.** Per this bullet's opening paragraph and `scripts/check_cadence_marker.py`'s own docstring, M1b's 2026-07-01 file carried the WRONG LABEL (`2026-06`) on RIGHT CONTENT (the July cycle's genuine output), and that label mismatch was independently "misread as the file being two cycles stale when in fact the July cycle had run fine" a month later at M4's gate. Folding a marker mismatch into the fatal `missing_dependency` path would make this gate mechanically reproduce that exact misdiagnosis instead of retiring it. **Disposition:** `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert_once('warning','<ID>','upstream_marker_mismatch','<upstream> marker <found> does not match the expected <expected> for the current period, though ops.run_log shows it completed in-period — verify the file content is genuinely current, not merely its label', '<JSON: upstream, found_marker, expected_marker, run_log_row>')` — a durable, emailed finding, same non-latching convention as `queue_item_stale` (category deliberately ABSENT from `ops.alert_policy`; resolve it the same evidence-based routine-owned way, on a later cycle where the same upstream's marker is found to match — **and here is that statement, because until 2026-08-20 this site named the close without ever writing it, and a close described but never stated is how a non-latching category quietly accumulates forever (the same gap the 2026-08-19 `artifact_version_drift` and `handoff_contract_unpinned` findings closed at their own sites):** on any later run of THIS routine where the same upstream's marker IS found to match the expected period, best-effort `UPDATE ops.alerts SET resolved=TRUE, resolved_ts=CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(), resolved_note='marker re-verified: <upstream> now stamps <found> == expected <expected>, <ID> run <today>' WHERE category='upstream_marker_mismatch' AND NOT resolved AND JSON_VALUE(payload,'$.upstream') = '<upstream>'` — scoped to the ONE upstream just re-verified, never a blanket clear of the category, and **never on age**: an aged-out row is indistinguishable from a genuinely re-verified one, the `phantom_run_completion` aging-alert trap this file documents elsewhere. Raise-time therefore MUST populate `payload.upstream`, which the `sp_raise_alert_once` call above already does) — and PROCEED with the run. Do not halt on this condition alone, and do not enter the DEPENDENCY-WAIT WINDOW for it: polling cannot fix a label that will not change no matter how many times it is re-read, so the window would only burn its budget before exhausting into a false halt.

## H-PRE-24 — A PROCEDURE THAT PASSED THE CI DRYRUN HAS NOT BEEN SHOWN TO RUN — SMOKECALL IT AFTER APPLYING IT LIVE (added

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 334) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

- **A PROCEDURE THAT PASSED THE CI DRY-RUN HAS NOT BEEN SHOWN TO RUN — SMOKE-CALL IT AFTER APPLYING IT LIVE (added 2026-10-04, W5 SPEC-DEFECT NOTICE INTAKE, closing OPS1's `procedure_body_unvalidated_until_first_call`, alert `c93a7bcb-bce1-464f-8c8e-722de76d4669`; binds ANY session that lands a `CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE`).** MEASURED 2026-09-14: `bigquery/235` rewrote `ops.sp_raise_connector_tool_drift` block (c) as a correlated `NOT EXISTS` over `state.connector_tool_inventory_stale`; the `CREATE` was accepted, `ci.yml`'s `warehouse-validation` dry-run passed it (a dry run validates the CREATE statement, not an invocation of the body), and it landed green but every `CALL` failed with "Correlated subqueries that reference other tables are not supported unless they can be de-correlated". Its only caller is a once-daily routine, so the first `CALL` in existence was OPS1's 2026-09-14 fire and one full operating day of the TOOL-INVENTORY DRIFT CHECK was lost (fixed in `bigquery/239`; De Morgan was tried first and failed identically). **The rule: the session that applies a changed procedure live must `CALL` it once, in the same session, before it logs `completed`, and name the outcome in its `<note>`.** Use the procedure's own no-op or idempotent path — the `sp_raise_*`/`sp_sq_*` family dedups on `(category, message)` and self-heals, so a real `CALL` is safe; for one with a non-idempotent side effect, run its body's `SELECT`/`INSERT … SELECT` halves standalone or call it with a read-only/zero argument. A `CALL` that errors means the landing is NOT done: fix it (a new numbered file) or `CREATE OR REPLACE` back to the prior body before the session ends. **Deliberately NOT adopted: a CI step that `CALL`s procedures.** `checks` holds no BigQuery identity and every added Actions minute is billed on EVERY push (CLAUDE.md push-discipline notes), so the cost lands ~290 times a month to cover a class that this one post-apply `CALL` already closes at the only moment it can matter; revisit only if a smoke-`CALL` omission is measured again.
- **Failure alerts (on any hard-stop).** Routine chat is unmonitored, so any condition that halts a routine or needs a human MUST be surfaced: `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert('critical', '<ID>', '<category>', '<one-line message>', '<JSON context>')` AND log the run `'halted'`. `alert_emailer.gs`'s 2-hourly poll of `ops.alerts` already delivers this by email — **no calendar event** (2026-07-09; the sole exception, a BigQuery-unreachable pre-flight, is handled separately above since BigQuery down means `sp_raise_alert` itself can't run). **As of 2026-09-08 the ‘raise an alert AND log halted’ pairing is MECHANICALLY BACKSTOPPED, not merely conventional prose**: `ops.sp_log_run` itself now raises a `routine_run_failed` `'warning'` on any `'failed'`/`'halted'` terminal row (bigquery/230_run_outcome_notification.sql), independent of whether the routine's own `sp_raise_alert` call above actually ran. The backstop is deliberately `'warning'`, never `'critical'` — an open critical feeds `state.trading_enabled`'s `blocking_criticals` (bigquery/107; the live gate definition since 2026-08-17 is `bigquery/176_decouple_embedding_health_from_trading_gate.sql`) and halts order staging fleet-wide, and a routine simply failing to complete is not on its own a capital-safety event, so this can never contribute to a trading halt. **It is a safety net, not a substitute for the routine's OWN cause-specific alert, which stays REQUIRED**: the backstop carries only the FACT that a run failed (routine, run_date, status, whatever `error_msg`/`<note>` happened to hold) — the routine's own alert below carries the DIAGNOSIS a human actually needs in order to act, and skipping it because "the backstop will catch it" leaves the owner a fact with no explanation. Hard-stops include: the §13 cash-tripwire >$1 unexplained residual (`cash_tripwire`); a Strategy C max-loss **dual-path disagreement** (closed-form vs Monte-Carlo diverge — a code-bug signal per Strategy.md, not a normal deferral; `dual_path`); `state.embedding_health.is_healthy = FALSE` after a decision write (`embedding`) **[NOT A HARD-STOP SINCE 2026-08-17 — correction appended 2026-09-29: `bigquery/176_decouple_embedding_health_from_trading_gate.sql` took embedding health out of the trading gate by owner decision, and raising it here as a `critical` would put it straight back in, because every open critical outside `trading_halted`/`staleness` counts in `blocking_criticals`. Call `ops.sp_embed_pending()` once, best-effort, and continue; a persistent failure already surfaces daily as the non-blocking `staleness` critical from `ops.sp_sq_daily_freshness_check`. See the EMBEDDING-LAG REPAIR bullet's 2026-09-29 correction]**; a required connector (IBKR / BigQuery) unreachable (`connector`); a **missed order confirmation** discovered by D3 (`missed_confirmation`, see D3 Calendar Hygiene); or any other unrecoverable state. (A normal deferral that resolves to its `conservative_default` is NOT a hard-stop — no alert.) **A hard-stop does NOT skip Session end (stated explicitly, landing-hardening 2026-07-29).** `'halted'`/`'failed'` is a *terminal log*, not an immediate exit: before writing it, perform §Branch and state propagation → "Session end" — commit all changes, push the assigned branch, verify with `git ls-remote` — exactly as a `'completed'` run would, so any file edits the run had already made reach `origin` instead of dying with the container. The only differences are the status logged and the `error_msg` attached. (A 2026-07-29 audit read all 33 slices and found every abort-capable gate it examined — connector pre-flight, the dependency gate, the upstream-output freshness gate, the same-day double-run guard, the arsenal kill-switch — positioned BEFORE any file edit, so today this is expected to be a no-op in practice; that was a systematic read, not an exhaustive proof over every conditional branch. It is stated anyway so that a mid-run hard-stop added *after* an edit step by some future revision cannot silently strand work, and so no routine has to infer the commit obligation from the run-logging template.)

## H-PRE-25 — OUTOFSCOPE FINDINGS — WHERE THEY GO, AND WHAT COUNTS AS A VENUE (added 20260828; binds ALL routines).

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 356) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

- **OUT-OF-SCOPE FINDINGS — WHERE THEY GO, AND WHAT COUNTS AS A VENUE (added 2026-08-28; binds ALL routines).**
  Every fleet trigger already carries the standing operator addendum: *"For an issue OUTSIDE this run's scope:
  fix it only if it BLOCKS this run's own job or the fix is a one-line correction; otherwise record it — an
  `events.queue_events` row or an `ops.alerts` info row naming the owning routine or surface — and move on."*
  That addendum is the binding rule; it lives in the trigger prompt (`scripts/routine_backup.py` `SCOPE_ADDENDUM`,
  mirrored onto all 33 fleet triggers and CI-enforced by that script's `check` (2)), which means it is NOT in this
  corpus and specifies only *record it*. This bullet is the missing half — the part a routine can actually read
  while it works, restated here because a rule that reaches a routine only through its prompt preamble, and never
  through the plan it executes from, gets honored inconsistently. Measured 2026-08-28: it was.
  - **A RUN-LOG NOTE AND A `reason` STRING ARE NOT VENUES.** The addendum names exactly two. On 2026-08-27 D2a
    found a real spec defect and recorded it only in an `ops.trading_control` `reason` string plus its own
    `ops.run_log` note — reaching no routine at all — the night after correctly filing a different finding as an
    `ops.alerts` info row (`sweep_recipient_view_drift`). Prose that no consumer reads is not a record; it is a
    diary. If the finding is worth stating, it is worth putting where something will pick it up.
  - **NAME A VERIFIED CONSUMER, NEVER AN ASSUMED ONE.** Before naming an owner, confirm that owner actually reads
    the venue you are writing to — SL2 pinned this for itself on 2026-08-27 ("AN OUT-OF-SECTION ESCALATION MUST
    NAME A VERIFIED CONSUMER"); it generalizes to every routine. Verify by reading the named routine's own section
    for a matching read, exactly as `state.queue_venue_claim_unwired` (`bigquery/160` + `bigquery/199`) does
    mechanically for the queue venue. This is not hypothetical: SL2's `premortem_preamble_stale` names W4, and W4's
    section contains no alert read at all.
  - **VENUE CHOICE.** Prefer `events.queue_events` when a lane with a real drainer fits the finding
    (`PENDING_ANALYSIS`→D2, `PENDING_REVIEW`→AR_att/AR_orc, `PENDING_DRAFT`→SL2, `PENDING_ROSTER`→SL5) — that venue
    has both drainers and a mechanical consumer-reality check. **THAT LANE→DRAINER MAP IS VERIFIED AT LANE
    GRANULARITY ONLY, SO CHECK THE DRAINER'S OWN PREDICATE BEFORE USING IT (added 2026-09-13, AR_att):**
    `PENDING_REVIEW`'s drainers act only on a row at `status='pending'` carrying a `payload.review_type` from the
    eight-value enum in §"Pending_Adversarial_Reviews.md — queue file schema", so a finding filed there at any other
    status or with no `review_type` reaches no drainer at all — measured on four such rows 2026-08-23..25, closed by
    their own owners rather than by the lane (AR_att's RESIDUAL SWEEP now names them instead of passing over them in
    silence). For a SPEC/DESIGN defect on a surface you do not own,
    no such lane exists: use an `ops.alerts` **`info`** row, which W5's SPEC-DEFECT NOTICE INTAKE now drains.
    Format, matching existing house practice: `severity='info'`, `source` = the DISCOVERING routine (never the
    owner), `category` = a snake_case noun phrase naming the defect class, and a message opening
    `OWNER: <file or surface> (nearest owning routine: <id>)` followed by what is wrong and what you did NOT do.
  - **BEFORE YOU FILE: CHECK FOR AN OPEN ROW ON THE SAME SURFACE, AND DO NOT MINT A LATCHING ROW FOR A
    NO-ACTION OBSERVATION (added 2026-09-14).** This rule previously specified only WHERE a finding goes — never
    whether anyone had already filed it, nor whether it should be filed at all — and both gaps are measurable on
    the board it feeds.
    **(a) DE-DUP FIRST.** Read `SELECT alert_id, source, category, message FROM ops.alerts WHERE severity='info'
    AND NOT resolved` before writing. If an open row already names your surface, add your evidence to THAT
    finding — a `sp_log_decision` entry citing its `alert_id` — instead of minting a second row. MEASURED
    2026-09-14: ~10-15% of the 49-row open board is rediscovery, including one case where W5 ROUTED
    `nomadic_borrow_blocked_unsignalled` as a queue workaround (explicitly not a fix) and D2 re-filed the
    identical gap ONE DAY later, and a five-way cluster in which OPS1, D2a, AR_att (twice) and D2 each
    independently filed the same MCP query-size confusion. Rediscovery is not the dominant driver of backlog
    growth, but it is the cheapest part of it to stop.
    **THE STRATEGY-BLIND PAIR (M1a, M1R) RUNS THIS READ METADATA-ONLY (added 2026-09-29, owner decision on
    alert `731e53d0-d341-4f44-babd-512e74308ee8`).** Open rows filed by non-blind routines carry the very
    substance those two must never see, in `message` and in `payload`, so for them this de-dup selects
    `alert_id, source, category, severity, alert_ts` ONLY and only over `source IN ('M1a','M1R')` — see the
    `ops.alerts` rule in M1a's Read access scope. A duplicate of another routine's finding costs one W5 merge
    and is the accepted price; every other routine reads this exactly as written above.
    **(b) A NO-ACTION OBSERVATION IS NOT A DEFECT NOTICE.** If at the moment of filing you already know nothing
    is broken and no change is owed — the `OWNER ACTION: none required; this is a record-and-refer finding`
    shape — write it to `events.decision_log` via `sp_log_decision`, NOT to `ops.alerts`. An `info` row here
    LATCHES by design and can then only be cleared by a W5 adjudication spending scarce budget to conclude what
    you already knew. This is ~8% of arrivals.
    **THIS IS NOT A QUALITY BAR ON GENUINE FINDINGS AND MUST NOT BECOME ONE.** 71% of the open board is real,
    mechanically-fixable defect, and the measured failure mode in this system is UNDER-reporting (the 2026-08-27
    diary-not-a-venue case above), not over-reporting. If you are unsure whether a finding is actionable, FILE
    IT — a redundant row costs one adjudication, a withheld one costs a defect nobody ever sees.
  - **THIS DOES NOT REOPEN "don't invent an `ops.alerts` category".** The `VISIBILITY — a suppressed run must not be
    silent` and `RUN-LOG GAP INTERPRETATION` bullets above still stand: a routine's own RUN-STATE narrative uses
    `sp_log_decision`, not a new alert category. The rule there is about not minting categories for ordinary
    run-state, and its stated hazard is that an unregistered category latches forever under `ops.alert_policy`'s
    fail-closed allowlist. A cross-surface defect notice is a different class and its latching is now the CORRECT
    behavior, because W5 owns the close — an evidence-bearing resolve, not an auto-age. Do NOT add these categories
    to `ops.alert_policy`'s auto-resolve allowlist: that would clear them without anyone having acted.
  - **A REFERRAL NEEDS A RETURN PATH, NOT JUST A SEND (added 2026-09-14, closing `referral_adjudication_no_return_path`;
    follows the same sequencing W5 applied to the sibling declared-surface referral, `13a4176e`, ADJUDICATED
    2026-08-30).** Filing a referral under the VENUE CHOICE bullet above is one-directional by default: nothing
    writes the adjudication back into the referring routine's own section, so that section can keep asserting a
    referral is open — or, worse, assert the opposite of live state — long after the named owner has ruled on it.
    SL3 hit this concretely on 2026-08-31: three of its own four open referrals had already been adjudicated by
    W5 on 2026-08-30 and its text still called all three open, and one claim had inverted — SL3's own block
    asserted `probe_funding_stalled` latched human-only with no `ops.alert_policy` row, on the exact day that
    policy row (`bigquery/204_probe_funding_stalled_alert_policy.sql`) went live naming SL3 itself as the resolve
    owner. **The fix is a convention, not a mechanism, and mechanizing it is deferred, matching `13a4176e`'s own
    sequencing exactly — survey, then correct routine by routine, then mechanize last, if ever:** any routine
    that refers a finding out must, before its own terminal step each fire, re-check `state.decision_log_current`
    for a later `ops-note`/`routine-outcome` adjudicating that referral and re-measure any live claim it rests
    on, folding the outcome into its own section the same fire — SL3's STEP 5 bullet ("BEFORE WRITING THE
    HEARTBEAT, RE-RESOLVE...") is the reference implementation. A one-time survey of which of the ~31 other
    referring routines' own sections currently assert an open referral that live `events.decision_log`/`ops.alerts`
    state already shows adjudicated or inverted — the exact SL3 shape — is real, bounded follow-on work, not
    started here and not owed from this edit, exactly as `13a4176e`'s own survey step was left as "its own unit
    of work"; until that survey runs, each routine adopts the STEP-5 convention the next time its own section is
    corrected for any reason. **Do NOT build a fleet-wide checker that derives open referrals and alarms on
    staleness ahead of the survey** — the same land-a-check-on-untested-history hazard CLAUDE.md warns about, and
    the same order already decided for the declared-surface gap.
