Record the result in that fire's `events.decision_log` (tag `re-resolve`) and its run note. Its own section holds at most ONE status line per open referral (`id | owner | status | date status last changed | alert_id/entry_id`): rewrite it only on a status change and delete it when closed. Never append dated per-fire entries or copy the old ledger. The condensed SL3 STEP 5 is the reference implementation. Each routine adopts this status-line convention the next time its section is corrected. A one-time survey of other routines' stale open-referral claims remains a separate bounded unit of work. **Do NOT build a fleet-wide checker that derives open referrals and alarms on staleness ahead of the survey** — first survey, then correct routine by routine, then mechanize if ever. Land any rule fix as the corrected RULE at its site: imperative text plus at most a one-clause reason. Put provenance (`alert_id`, category, measurements, before/after counts, commit) in `resolved_note` and an `events.decision_log` ops-note, never in the plan. A short back-pointer such as `[ref alert 3f2a…]` is sufficient. Never add measured-date narratives, incident timelines or correction-of-correction paragraphs to `Claude_Task_Plan.md`; replace superseded text in place. Git history is the record. *(Pre-2026-10-30 history and evidence for this rule: `ops/plan_history/00_preamble.md` § H-PRE-25.)*

## H-PRE-26 — Crafting an order (equity/ETF). When a routine stages an order: (1) resolve the contract_id (use the cached id

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 485) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

**Crafting an order (equity/ETF).** When a routine stages an order: (1) resolve the `contract_id` (use the cached id from the **Cached `contract_id`s** list in this section below, else `search_contracts` selecting the US primary listing — `country_code` US, primary exchange, exact symbol, STK/ETF section); (2) pull a **realtime** `get_price_snapshot` (bid/ask, last). **All orders are MARKET orders** (`order_type='MARKET'`, `time_in_force='DAY'` — owner directive 2026-07-21; there are no limit orders in this system). Record the live **reference** price (last, or bid/ask mid) in the staged payload's `limit_price` field for cash-reservation purposes **only** — it is NOT sent as an order limit; if no live quote is available (empty bid/ask pre-market), use last, or defer if neither is available (there is simply no reference price to record — not a liquidity judgment); (3) **NOMADIC-BORROW CHECK (owner directive 2026-08-11, redesigned same day; Operating_Protocols.md §16 NOMADIC STRATEGY CAPITAL; schema `bigquery/167_nomadic_capital.sql` as fixed by `bigquery/168_nomadic_capital_fixes.sql`) — SCOPE FIRST, then a cheap pre-filter.** **SCOPE: this check runs ONLY on a craft that CONSUMES cash — a new entry or an add-tranche. It is SKIPPED ENTIRELY on every EXIT, TRIM, partial-sell, cover, or park-cover craft**, which liquidate a position INTO cash and can never need funding; running it on a sell would compare a sell's notional against `available_funds` and "borrow" a shortfall that does not exist. (Do not gate on the literal `side` field: an options combo entry can carry a SELL-tagged leg and still consume cash. The test is what the craft DOES — open/add exposure vs close/reduce it.) Then the cheap pre-filter, for a cash-consuming craft only: `SELECT is_low_frequency_by_design FROM state.strategy_declared_frequency WHERE strategy_code=<strategy>` (a 5-row static table — near-zero cost). If FALSE (A/B/E on the current roster — every craft today), **skip this entire step**; this is a PERMANENT classification, not conditioned on recent activity, so this one lookup is the whole test. If TRUE (currently C/D, classified NOMADIC — a strategy holding no exclusive standing capital by design), compute this order's estimated notional (`qty × ref_price` from the snapshot just pulled) and compare to the strategy's current `available_funds` (`analytics.strategy_nav`). If notional exceeds `available_funds`: `SELECT * FROM analytics.fn_nomadic_capital_restore_plan(p_strategy => <strategy>, p_amount_needed => <notional - available_funds>)` — sources the requested amount (size is the AI's own seven-factor-justified call; this function never second-guesses or caps it), pro-rata from the other enabled non-nomadic strategies' `available_funds`, clamped only by their total capacity. **Do NOT craft if EITHER: the function returns no rows; OR any row reads `control_enabled = FALSE`; OR any row reads `is_fully_funded = FALSE`.** That last one is load-bearing — `plan_total` is the CLAMPED sourceable figure and `requested_amount` is what you asked for, so a partial pull would otherwise leave the order under-funded while every other signal looked healthy. In all three cases log why via `CALL ops.sp_log_decision(...)` and treat as un-stageable this session (retry next run — a capital-availability defer, not a thesis rejection). Otherwise, write the atomic $0-sum `events.cash_flows` double-entry on one flow_date tagged `source='nomadic_capital_restore'` — one positive row for `<strategy>` at `SUM(pull_amount)` (which equals `plan_total` exactly; the TVF applies a last-donor-absorbs-the-residual rule, so do NOT re-round it), one negative row per distinct `donor_strategy` at its own `pull_amount` — and `CALL ops.sp_log_decision(..., entry_type='capital-allocation', ...)` with `fields` carrying `trigger='nomadic_capital_restore'`, `conviction='MECHANICAL'`, `conviction_pct=null`, and a `rationale` naming the amount pulled and from which donors. Then continue to the ORDER-GUARD CHECK below against the now-larger `available_funds`. (4) **ORDER-GUARD CHECK (market-only + malformed-input sanity ONLY, owner directive 2026-07-22 — ALL liquidity and sizing pre-trade rails are removed; `bigquery/104_strip_pretrade_rails.sql` supersedes the 2026-07-21 expected-shortfall liquidity gate and every rail before it)** — `SELECT * FROM analytics.fn_order_guard(<strategy>, <side>, <qty>, <ref_price>, 'MARKET')`; if `passed = FALSE`, do NOT craft — `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert_once('critical', <routine>, 'order_guard_block', '<routine> — order guard rejected a staged order: <guard-reasons>; see payload for the order fields', <JSON>)` (**MESSAGE PINNED 2026-09-07:** `<reasons joined>` named no join order, so the same rejection permutes between runs and re-raises — on a CRITICAL. `<guard-reasons>` is the DISTINCT reason codes, sorted ASCENDING, joined by a single comma with NO spaces) and skip (the guard only rejects a non-MARKET order_type or a non-positive qty/ref_price — a rejection means the order is malformed, never a liquidity/pricing judgment; whether to trade a name at all, at any price or size, is now the AI's thesis/market-conditions judgment plus the owner's IBKR confirm-tap, not a mechanical pre-trade gate). If `passed = TRUE`, call `create_order_instruction(contract_id, side, quantity, order_type='MARKET', time_in_force='DAY')` (no `limit_price` argument transmitted) — **`time_in_force` always `DAY`, never GTC** (the connector has no modify/amend endpoint, so a persist-and-wait order is re-crafted fresh as a DAY market order each session; Operating_Protocols.md §11) and capture `{id, url}`; (5) record the instruction `id` in the `state.open_orders` staged-order row (`events.queue_events`, queue `ORDER_STAGED`), including `guard_passed`/`guard_reasons` from the preceding ORDER-GUARD CHECK, in the payload (self-improvement audit ITEM 15, 2026-07-11 — the guard result embedded so the payload itself proves the guard ran; every order craft in this system, including the §13.E park sweep/cover, is gated by an order-guard check, so this applies uniformly — a craft site that omits this leaves its `ORDER_STAGED` payload indistinguishable from a genuine guard bypass to `daily_staging_cap_check.sql`'s `order_guard_omitted` CRITICAL; the daily-staging-cap backstop now recomputes the market-only + sanity guard verdict straight from the payload's own side/qty/ref_price — there are no liquidity inputs left to store); (6) put `url` + summary + `id` into chat — `create_order_instruction`'s own IBKR notification is the human-facing confirm surface for a craftable equity/ETF order (2026-07-09 convention, no calendar event); a `[Claude] Confirm order` calendar event is created only for a genuinely non-craftable/manual-entry order (the fallback below). If a staged order is superseded before the operator confirms, call `delete_order_instruction(id)`.

## H-PRE-27 — Crafting an order (options — Strategy C / A/C options theses; selfimprovement audit ITEM 13, 20260711). create

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 489) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

**Crafting an order (options — Strategy C / A/C options theses; self-improvement audit ITEM 13, 2026-07-11).** `create_order_instruction` supports single-leg Options (OPT) and OPT–OPT combos/spreads directly (verified against the tool's own documentation — the repo previously, incorrectly, documented options as connector-uncraftable; that mischaracterization is corrected here and everywhere else it appeared). (1) Resolve each leg's contract via `get_option_data` (after `get_option_parameters` resolves the expiration id) — capture `call_contract_id_ex`/`put_contract_id_ex` verbatim. (2) **Single-leg** (a long call/put): call `create_order_instruction(contract_id_ex=<that leg's id>, side, quantity=<contracts>, order_type='MARKET', time_in_force='DAY')` directly (no `limit_price` argument transmitted) — same pattern as equity, quantity is CONTRACTS not shares. (3) **Multi-leg** (spreads/condors/butterflies — Strategy C's defined-risk structures): call `get_combo_identifier(legs=[{contract_id_ex, size: +N for BUY / -N for SELL}, ...])` FIRST (OPT legs only — never pass FOP ids) to obtain a combo `contract_id_ex`, then `create_order_instruction(contract_id_ex=<the combo id from get_combo_identifier>, side, quantity, order_type='MARKET', time_in_force='DAY')` (no `limit_price` argument transmitted). (4) **ORDER-GUARD CHECK, options-specific — before calling `create_order_instruction` for ANY options order:** compute the structure's max loss via `c_options_math.py`'s dual-path verification (`verify_max_loss_dual_path` on `max_loss_closed_form` vs `max_loss_monte_carlo` — refuses to proceed on an `UnboundedMaxLossError`, i.e. never craft an undefined-risk structure). **That BASE figure is what you pass as `max_loss_dollars`**, and it already satisfies Strategy C's cascade-inclusive eligibility rule, because `cascade_max_loss` is provably **≤** the base figure for every structure this module can build: for the binding short leg, `cascade_loss(mark) = −pnl_at_expiration(mark) + (payoff of any OTHER short legs at mark)`, and every short leg's payoff is ≤ 0 by construction, so `cascade_loss ≤ max over all prices of −pnl_at_expiration = max_loss_closed_form`. Verified empirically 2026-08-10 over a 16,280-structure randomized sweep (credit call/put spreads, iron condors, both butterfly types; DTE 3–45d; vols 10–120%; implied move 1–200%): zero counter-examples, max observed `cascade/base` = 1.0000000022 (floating-point equality, never a real excess). So `total_max_loss = max(base, cascade) ≡ base`, and the cascade term **cannot** change which orders pass. **CASCADE CONSISTENCY CHECK — non-blocking, and never a precondition for crafting.** When an at-entry implied-move figure is available, compute `implied_move_full_horizon = <at-entry IV, already required by the classical-method-delegation list> × sqrt(days_to_expiration / 365)` and call `cascade_max_loss(structure, implied_move_full_horizon=<that>)`; record it alongside the base figure. If it ever comes back **greater** than the base figure, the proof above has been violated — do NOT craft, and `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert_once('critical', <routine>, 'cascade_exceeds_base', ...)`. If no implied-move figure is available, SKIP the check and say so in `<note>` — do not block, and do not invent a value: `implied_move_full_horizon` is a REQUIRED argument with no default, so calling it unsourced raises `TypeError`, and a non-positive/NaN value raises `ValueError`, either of which would abort the craft *before* the guard ever runs. (A long-call/long-put structure short-circuits to `0.0` before that validation, so only multi-leg structures are exposed to it.) **NOMADIC-BORROW CHECK — same mechanism as the equity/ETF crafting step above, keyed on `max_loss_dollars` instead of `qty × ref_price`.** **SCOPE, exactly as in the equity step: cash-consuming crafts ONLY — a new structure or an add. SKIP IT ENTIRELY when closing, rolling-to-close, or scaling out of an existing structure.** Judge that by what the craft does to exposure, NOT by the combo's net `side`: a net-CREDIT spread ENTRY is opening exposure and still needs funding (its Capital at Risk is `max_loss_dollars`, not the premium received), while a buy-to-close of a short leg is an exit and needs none. Then the cheap pre-filter: `SELECT is_low_frequency_by_design FROM state.strategy_declared_frequency WHERE strategy_code=<strategy>` (skip the rest entirely if FALSE — A/B/E on the current roster). If TRUE (currently C/D — and C is options-only, so this is the check's primary live site), and `max_loss_dollars` (the structure's Capital at Risk, just computed above — the correct funding basis for a defined-risk structure whether debit or credit) exceeds current `available_funds` (`analytics.strategy_nav`), source the shortfall via `analytics.fn_nomadic_capital_restore_plan(p_strategy => <strategy>, p_amount_needed => <max_loss_dollars - available_funds>)` and write the tagged `source='nomadic_capital_restore'` double-entry exactly as specified in the equity/ETF step — including its three do-NOT-craft conditions (no rows / `control_enabled = FALSE` / **`is_fully_funded = FALSE`**) and the same `ops.sp_log_decision` call. **UNVERIFIED-ON-FIRST-FILL FLAG (audit 2026-08-11, `bigquery/168` header "KNOWN LIMITATION"):** a multi-leg combo stages under ONE `position_key`, but `events.position_events`/`state.current_positions` are schema-singular and latest-wins with no coalescing — so IF IBKR reports the fill as separate per-leg executions, only the last leg's OPEN row may survive, understating committed capital and inflating `available_funds` for this very check. The sweep GATE is safe regardless (every defined-risk structure has a long leg, which keeps `open_positions ≥ 1` for the structure's whole life), but the borrow/sweep AMOUNT is not yet verified. On C's FIRST multi-leg fill, confirm how Step 0 reconciled the legs and record the finding. Record the live reference premium (mid, or last) for cash-reservation/notional purposes only — it is NOT sent as an order limit; then `SELECT * FROM analytics.fn_order_guard_options(<strategy>, <side>, <contracts>, <ref_premium>, <max_loss_dollars>, 'MARKET')` (`bigquery/104_strip_pretrade_rails.sql` — market-only + malformed-input sanity + the RETAINED defined-risk rail only; every liquidity leg, open-interest floor, and the sizing cap are removed, owner directive 2026-07-22). If `passed = FALSE`, do NOT craft — `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert_once('critical','<ID>','order_guard_block', '<routine> — order guard rejected a staged order: <guard-reasons>; see payload for the order fields', <JSON>)` (**MESSAGE PINNED 2026-09-07:** `<reasons joined>` named no join order, so the same rejection permutes between runs and re-raises — on a CRITICAL. `<guard-reasons>` is the DISTINCT reason codes, sorted ASCENDING, joined by a single comma with NO spaces) and treat as un-stageable this session (the guard now only rejects a non-MARKET order_type, non-positive contracts/ref_premium, or an undefined/non-positive `max_loss_dollars` — never a liquidity call). (5) Record the instruction `id` in `state.open_orders` (`ORDER_STAGED`) and put `url` + summary + `id` into chat — `create_order_instruction`'s own IBKR notification is the human-facing surface for a craftable options order, exactly as for equity (no calendar event, 2026-07-09 convention). **Fallback (genuinely non-craftable only):** if `get_combo_identifier` rejects the structure (a mixed OPT/FOP combo, or a structure it cannot resolve) or the security type is FOP/FUT and not single-leg, fall back to the manual-entry text order block in a `[Claude] Confirm order` calendar event (below) — this is now the CONTINGENCY path, not the default for every option.

On C's FIRST multi-leg fill, confirm how Step 0 reconciled the legs and record the finding. Record the live reference premium (mid, or last) for cash-reservation/notional purposes only — it is NOT sent as an order limit; then `SELECT * FROM analytics.fn_order_guard_options(<strategy>, <side>, <contracts>, <ref_premium>, <max_loss_dollars>, 'MARKET')` (`bigquery/104_strip_pretrade_rails.sql` — market-only + malformed-input sanity + the RETAINED defined-risk rail only; every liquidity leg, open-interest floor, and the sizing cap are removed, owner directive 2026-07-22). If `passed = FALSE`, do NOT craft — `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert_once('critical','<ID>','order_guard_block', '<routine> — order guard rejected a staged order: <guard-reasons>; see payload for the order fields', <JSON>)` `<guard-reasons>` is the DISTINCT reason codes, sorted ASCENDING, joined by a single comma with NO spaces; then treat as un-stageable this session (the guard now only rejects a non-MARKET order_type, non-positive contracts/ref_premium, or an undefined/non-positive `max_loss_dollars` — never a liquidity call). (5) Record the instruction `id` in `state.open_orders` (`ORDER_STAGED`) and put `url` + summary + `id` into chat — `create_order_instruction`'s own IBKR notification is the human-facing surface for a craftable options order, exactly as for equity (no calendar event, 2026-07-09 convention). **Fallback (genuinely non-craftable only):** if `get_combo_identifier` rejects the structure (a mixed OPT/FOP combo, or a structure it cannot resolve) or the security type is FOP/FUT and not single-leg, fall back to the manual-entry text order block in a `[Claude] Confirm order` calendar event (below) — this is now the CONTINGENCY path, not the default for every option. *(Pre-2026-10-30 history and evidence for this rule: `ops/plan_history/00_preamble.md` § H-PRE-27.)*

## H-PRE-28 — Reconciling fills (D2a Step 0, daily, idempotent by trade_id; owner as of the 20260709 cutover — this

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 493) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

**Reconciling fills (D2a Step 0, daily, idempotent by `trade_id`; owner as of the 2026-07-09 cutover — this
summary previously still said "D2 Step 0" here, which is why the fix below landed as dead prose for six weeks;
the AUTHORITATIVE copy of the alert-resolve clauses is now inline in D2a's own STEP 0 bullet, Claude_Task_Plan.md
"D2a. Broker Reconcile & Snapshot" — this paragraph is a non-canonical operational summary, per this section's
own header above, and must be kept in sync with that copy, not treated as a second independent source of truth).**
Read `get_account_trades` over a multi-day window floored at DAYS_7 but widened to `GREATEST(7 days, days
since D2a's own last successful completion)` — kept in sync with the canonical STEP 0 copy above, per this
routine's own header (owner directive 2026-07-25 CATCH-UP EVIDENCE WINDOW; fills are idempotent on
`trade_id`, so widening is safe). For each fill whose `trade_id` is not already
recorded in `events.trade_fills`: write exact price / size / `commission` / `realized_pnl` / `trade_time`
(`INSERT INTO events.trade_fills`); flip ORDER-STAGED→OPEN or exit-pending→CLOSED via an `events.position_events`
row (a SELL fill that does NOT zero the (strategy,ticker) position is instead a PARTIAL-sell — apply the sold
shares FIFO across the position's open tranches and write a CLOSE for each fully-consumed tranche plus an
`ADJUST`/`status='OPEN'` row for the partially-consumed tranche at the FIFO boundary, per D2a Step 0's own
partial-sell reconciliation bullet, the authoritative copy — owner directive 2026-07-22); update strategy sector counts / KL events; record the fill against the position's decision via `CALL
ops.sp_log_decision(...)` (`events.decision_log`). **Resolve any open `termination_close_staged` alert this fill
satisfies (self-improvement audit ITEM 17, 2026-07-11):** `UPDATE ops.alerts SET resolved=TRUE, resolved_ts=CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(), resolved_note='closed by fill <trade_id>' WHERE category='termination_close_staged' AND NOT resolved AND JSON_VALUE(payload,'$.instruction_id') = <this fill's originating instruction_id>` (match via `state.open_orders.instruction_id`, same as the ORDER-STAGED→CLOSED flip above). **Resolve any open `position_reconciliation_lag` alert this fill closes (root-cause fix, 2026-07-20; match condition corrected 2026-07-21 — the original strategy-scoped match never fires for a name that turns out to split across multiple strategy buckets, since D1 guesses a strategy attribution before reconciliation runs; 2026-07-21's ISRG is the live example: D1 assumed a strategy-D duplicate re-craft, but the reconciled fill correctly opened an independent strategy-B position):** `UPDATE ops.alerts SET resolved=TRUE, resolved_ts=CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(), resolved_note='closed by fill <trade_id>: now reconciled into state.current_positions' WHERE category='position_reconciliation_lag' AND NOT resolved AND JSON_VALUE(payload,'$.ticker') = <this fill's ticker>` — match by ticker ONLY, never also require `payload.strategy` equality. Take realized P&L from the connector's `realized_pnl` field — never infer it. Aggregate exchange-split partial fills by `order_id`. Then refresh live marks/cash from `get_account_positions` + `get_account_summary` + `get_account_balances`, and note still-working orders from `get_account_orders`.

## H-PRE-29 — Sizing and analysis on live data. The positionsizing base is the perstrategy subportfolio NAV, read from analy

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 513) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

**Sizing and analysis on live data.** The position-sizing base is the **per-strategy sub-portfolio NAV**, read from `analytics.strategy_nav` (~$4,700–4,990/strategy as of 2026-08-05; the figure moves with deposits and P&L — **always read it, never reuse a number quoted here**) — NOT `get_account_summary` net-liquidation, which is the whole-account figure (~$19,583 as of 2026-08-05, after that day's operator deposit roughly doubled the account = all five sub-portfolios + the park, §13 — SGOV historically, VOO from the 2026-07-15 cutover forward) and oversizes the position by the ratio of the two (≈4× as of 2026-08-05; the multiple moves with the roster's funded count, so derive it, do not memorise it); net-liq / `available_funds` / `buying_power` are for execution-feasibility only (does the entry settle), never the sizing base. **The BASE is what this rule fixes; the FRACTION is not fixed** — as of Strategy.md Rev 43 / `Experiment_Parameters.md` rev 18 (owner directive 2026-07-28) there is no flat 2%: the AI sets each thesis's Capital-at-Risk budget, justified against the seven-factor list and adversarially attacked on size~~, bounded by the per-name (≤10% CaR, summed across tranches) and per-strategy-deployed (≤75% CaR) envelopes~~ **[owner directive 2026-08-05 — BOTH envelopes RETIRED (`Experiment_Parameters.md` rev 19). There is NO numeric ceiling on a thesis's budget at any level; the seven-factor justification and the adversarial attack on size are now the whole of the sizing discipline.]**. **LIVE-RAMP CAP (rev 20, 2026-10-19, owner directive — LIVE_RAMP_DESIGN.md section 4).** For a strategy whose `state.strategy_roster.current_state = 'PROBE'`, read `SELECT score, deployable_cap_usd, headroom_usd FROM state.strategy_ramp WHERE strategy_code = <strategy>` BEFORE the seven-factor call: the thesis notional may not exceed `headroom_usd` — the cap bounds the strategy's TOTAL deployed market value, and the seven-factor budget sets the size within that room, never above it. No row (a PROBE strategy without `ops.strategy_ramp_params`) reads as headroom 0: fail closed, do not craft, raise nothing here (SL5 branch (1) owns the missing row). If `headroom_usd` is below the cost-viability minimum for one round trip at the cost rail, do not craft — record the GO as staged-but-capped via `CALL ops.sp_log_decision(...)` citing the score and cap, the same idempotent re-check pattern as the entry-staging gate above. ADOPTED strategies carry no cap. Capital for a capped entry is sourced exactly as below (own `available_funds`, then `analytics.fn_nomadic_capital_restore_plan`).

## H-PRE-30 — RECORD THE CHOSEN BUDGET AS STRUCTURED DATA, not only as prose (added 20260805). Every events.decision_log ro

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 515) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

  **RECORD THE CHOSEN BUDGET AS STRUCTURED DATA, not only as prose (added 2026-08-05).** Every `events.decision_log` row that commits to a size — `entry_type` `thesis-construction`, `action-conversion`, and `add-candidate-review` when an add is actually staged — MUST carry these keys in its `fields` JSON, in addition to the prose justification in `body_md`:
  - `car_pct` — the chosen Capital-at-Risk budget as a percentage of that strategy's own sub-portfolio NAV (a number, e.g. `3.5`, not a string, not a fraction)
  - `car_usd` — the same budget in dollars, i.e. `car_pct/100 × analytics.strategy_nav.nav` at craft time
  - `sizing_base_usd` — the denominator actually used. This is what makes the wrong-base tripwire above auditable after the fact rather than only at craft time.
  - `car_basis` — how CaR is DEFINED for this instrument, one of `long_notional` (no stop-loss, so the honest worst case is total loss), `short_notional_x_stop`, or `options_max_loss`
  **Why this is mandatory now when it was not before.** Under the flat 2% rule the size was a constant, and under the Rev 43 envelopes it was bounded — in both regimes the number was recoverable without recording it. With both envelopes retired, **the chosen size is the central experimental variable of this whole system**, and as of 2026-08-05 not one `thesis-construction` or `action-conversion` row since Rev 43 carried a queryable budget: `car_pct`, `risk_budget_pct`, `capital_at_risk` and `size_pct` were NULL on every one. That makes "did the larger bets actually pay?" unanswerable without parsing prose — the single question free sizing most needs to be able to answer about itself. **This is a RECORD-ONLY contract: nothing reads these fields as a gate, and no threshold attaches to any of them.** The realised-concentration counterpart is `analytics.strategy_concentration` (`bigquery/140`), which measures the book rather than the intent; the two answer different questions and disagreeing is informative, not an error. `analytics.strategy_nav.sizing_base_2pct` is a **legacy reference column**, not the budget. **Sanity tripwire (re-based AGAIN 2026-08-05 — it must test the BASE, never a fraction): before staging, confirm the denominator you sized off EQUALS that strategy's own `analytics.strategy_nav.nav`. If the base you used is at or near the whole-account net-liquidation figure instead, the wrong base was used — STOP and recompute off the sub-portfolio.** **[CLARIFIED 2026-09-14, closing spec-defect notice `0a33539c` (`sizing_tripwire_undiscriminating_for_E`) — the two tests above are NOT independent triggers; proximity to whole-account NLV is a PROXY for "wrong base," not a second freestanding STOP condition. When one strategy legitimately holds nearly all standing capital, its own correct base is itself at/near NLV by construction, and the two tests literally disagree for it. The IDENTITY test governs: STOP only when the base you sized off does NOT equal that strategy's own `analytics.strategy_nav.nav`; proximity to NLV with the identity check passing is not grounds to stop. Verified live 2026-09-14: Strategy E's `analytics.strategy_nav.nav` was $15,368.39 against account NLV of $15,713.01 (97.8%) because A/B/D are capital-disabled, C is nomadic and therefore an ineligible sweep recipient (D2a `regime_sweep_blocked`), and E's activation has flip-flopped repeatedly (`events.regime_events`, `div-E-202605-1` through `div-E-202608-1`) without its capital ever moving elsewhere — so this recurs, it is not a one-off. This tripwire is prose-only; no `bigquery/*.sql` or `scripts/*.py` mechanically enforces it.]** ~~a computed single-name entry exceeding ~10% of the sub-portfolio~~ **[RETIRED 2026-08-05 — this fraction half of the tripwire is now actively WRONG and must not be reinstated. With both CaR envelopes retired there is no illegitimate fraction: a deliberately concentrated thesis may be any share of its sub-portfolio up to 100%, so a >10% test would STOP correct behaviour — precisely the failure the 2026-07-28 revision re-based this tripwire to avoid, repeated one bound later. Only the wrong-BASE half survives, and it is the half that ever detected the real error.]** The still-older "over ~$50 / >3%" form was retired for the same reason at Rev 43. Use `get_account_positions` for exact current holdings; use `get_price_snapshot` for LIVE quotes and convergence-target checks, and **`get_price_history` with `outside_rth=false` for any CLOSE-TO-CLOSE measurement** — the two are not interchangeable, and this line used to imply they were (fixed 2026-08-05, `d1_measurement_methodology_gap`). Evening-slot routines see AFTER-HOURS prints in a snapshot's `last`, and a snapshot's prior-close field can itself trail a session, so a close taken from a snapshot is wrong in two independent ways. Decide by what the measurement MEANS: a current price at/through a threshold → snapshot; a session-over-session move → daily bar. Operating_Protocols.md §19 PRICE BASIS is binding for the research screens. Web quotes are a fallback only when the connector lacks the instrument.

### Pass 2 input — q0126, 2026-10-30

If the base you used is at or near the whole-account net-liquidation figure instead, the wrong base was used — STOP and recompute off the sub-portfolio.** **[CLARIFIED 2026-09-14, closing spec-defect notice `0a33539c` (`sizing_tripwire_undiscriminating_for_E`) — the two tests above are NOT independent triggers; proximity to whole-account NLV is a PROXY for "wrong base," not a second freestanding STOP condition. This tripwire is prose-only; no `bigquery/*.sql` or `scripts/*.py` mechanically enforces it.]** ~~a computed single-name entry exceeding ~10% of the sub-portfolio~~ **[RETIRED 2026-08-05 — this fraction half of the tripwire is now actively WRONG and must not be reinstated. With both CaR envelopes retired there is no illegitimate fraction: a deliberately concentrated thesis may be any share of its sub-portfolio up to 100%, so a >10% test would STOP correct behaviour — precisely the failure the 2026-07-28 revision re-based this tripwire to avoid, repeated one bound later. Only the wrong-BASE half survives, and it is the half that ever detected the real error.]** The still-older "over ~$50 / >3%" form was retired for the same reason at Rev 43. Use `get_account_positions` for exact current holdings; use `get_price_snapshot` for LIVE quotes and convergence-target checks, and **`get_price_history` with `outside_rth=false` for any CLOSE-TO-CLOSE measurement** — the two are not interchangeable. Evening-slot routines see AFTER-HOURS prints in a snapshot's `last`, and a snapshot's prior-close field can itself trail a session, so a close taken from a snapshot is wrong in two independent ways. Decide by what the measurement MEANS: a current price at/through a threshold → snapshot; a session-over-session move → daily bar. Operating_Protocols.md §19 PRICE BASIS is binding for the research screens. Web quotes are a fallback only when the connector lacks the instrument. *(Pre-2026-10-30 history and evidence for this rule: `ops/plan_history/00_preamble.md` § H-PRE-30.)*

## H-PRE-31 — get_account_positions.market_price is a POSITION MARK, not a settled close, and can be several sessions stale

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 528) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

- **`get_account_positions.market_price` is a POSITION MARK, not a settled close, and can be several sessions stale.** Measured by D1 on 2026-09-07, a US market holiday with the last session 2026-09-04: `get_account_positions` served GOOGL at **335.00 — the 2026-09-01 close, THREE SESSIONS STALE** — against a true 09-04 close of 338.46. In the same run `get_price_snapshot` returned AMZN at 258.30 carrying **`is_close: false`** (a live-looking tick, stamped 2026-09-07T22:00:01Z, on a closed market) against a true 09-04 close of 258.51, while all eight other snapshots that run carried `is_close: true`. Only `get_price_history` (`step=ONE_DAY`, `outside_rth=false`) gave the correct close on every name. **So: never read a close out of `get_account_positions`, and when a snapshot is used at all, READ `is_close` — it is the field that distinguishes a settled print from a tick, and it is easy to miss because it is usually true.** `get_account_positions` remains correct for what it is for: exact current holdings and quantities. D2a already sources `events.daily_marks` from `get_price_history` by its own spec, so the known exposure is small — but **no one has swept the fleet for other position-mark or snapshot readers**, and this bullet is the record that the sweep has not been done rather than a claim that it is clean.
- **ASSERT `low <= close <= high` ON ANY IBKR DAILY BAR YOU CONSUME AS A CLOSE, AND PREFER AN ALREADY-INGESTED VALUE OVER A RE-PULL.** IBKR served a structurally impossible `^VIX` daily bar: the 2026-09-04 bar (contract_id 13455763, `security_type='IND'`, `exchange='CBOE'` — see the native-exchange bullet below) returned **`close=15.30` against its own `high=14.58` and `low=13.80`** — a close 0.72 ABOVE its own high — while every other bar in the same 21-bar pull was well formed. **The bar MUTATED after the fact:** the 2026-09-06 D1 park record cites the identical call and pinned 14.53 for that session, and `events.signal_marks` independently holds 14.53 from FMP; 14.53 sits inside the bar's own range and 15.30 does not. Third corroboration: against the well-formed 2026-09-08 close of 15.72, 14.53 implies +8.19%, matching an independent same-day report of VIX up ~8%, whereas 15.30 implies +2.75%, which no source carries. **The consequence is not cosmetic:** `state.park_axis_daily`'s volatility axis tests `VIX > 15 AND VIX > its own 20d SMA`, so the corrupt bar would have scored 09-04 as ALREADY standing defensive, when read correctly the axis ENTERED defensive on 09-08 — exactly the standing-vs-entry distinction the de-risk increase gate and the DE-RISK EVIDENCE CARDINALITY rule turn on. The 2026-09-08 D1 run caught it and substituted 14.53; a session reading the series naively would not have. **Where `events.signal_marks` / `state.signal_marks_curated` already holds a historical close, read it from there rather than re-pulling the bar** — the warehouse value is the one a prior run validated, and a re-pull can return a value that has since mutated.

## H-PRE-32 — Rich marketdata fields (use them wherever they sharpen a decision). get_price_snapshot exposes far more than l

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 531) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

**Rich market-data fields (use them wherever they sharpen a decision).** `get_price_snapshot` exposes far more than last/bid/ask:
- *Instrument eligibility:* `avg-90d-usd-volume` (when populated) gives dollar ADV for the Strategy B criterion-1 ≥$10M liquidity gate; it can be omitted by the API, so if absent derive ADV from `get_price_history` (volume × close). `misc-statistics` gives 13/26/52-week high/low.
- *Pre-event-rally / momentum context (B sub-pattern 3 + criterion-2 disproportion):* `year-to-date-change` + the 52-week range (`misc-statistics`) + returns derived from `get_price_history` quantify how much of a move was a pre-print rally already absorbing the narrative. (The `cumulative-perf-*` fields are ETF/fund-oriented and come back empty for most single stocks — do not rely on them for equities.)
- *Volatility context:* `implied-vol`, `implied-volatility-percentile`, `historical-vol` gauge whether a post-event move is large relative to the name's own vol regime.
- *Options analytics (A/C options theses):* `implied-vol`, `option-midpoint-iv`, `option-volume`, `option-open-interest`, `underlying-today/avg-option-volume`. The connector supplies both the data to BUILD and size the options thesis AND crafts the resulting order (single-leg + OPT–OPT combos — self-improvement audit ITEM 13, 2026-07-11, corrected from a prior "cannot craft options orders" mischaracterization).
- Always pull `get_price_history` with `include_corporate_actions: true` so splits / special dividends are surfaced and never masquerade as price moves — critical for the B criterion-1 close-to-close magnitude gate and convergence-target derivation, and for attributing account drift in D2 Step 0.
- **AN INDEX (`security_type='IND'`), FUTURE OR FUTURES-OPTION CALL MUST PASS THE CONTRACT'S NATIVE `exchange` — OMITTED, IT DEFAULTS TO SMART, WHICH DOES NOT ROUTE THEM, AND THE CONNECTOR ANSWERS WITH A NON-SPECIFIC "Details currently unavailable" THAT READS LIKE AN OUTAGE (added 2026-10-19, interactive owner session triaging the 2026-10-19 alert emails, closing D1's `routine_run_warning` `ibkr_vix_history_unavailable`, alert `6c7a05d0-1392-42e0-baa8-11cb98745502`).** Take `exchange` verbatim from the instrument's `search_contracts` row (for a future or futures option, from the exchange its futures-search or `get_option_parameters` result reports) (`^VIX` contract_id 13455763 and SPX 416904 are both `CBOE`) and pass it on every `get_price_history` AND `get_price_snapshot` call for that instrument. MEASURED 2026-10-19 with every argument identical except `exchange`: with it omitted (or set to SMART) both indices returned `Details currently unavailable. Please try again later…` from `get_price_history` (ONE_WEEK, ONE_MONTH and step_count 5 alike) and an empty `{}` from `get_price_snapshot` (or only `top-status` `REJECT` when that field is requested); with `exchange='CBOE'` both returned full OHLC bars and a `last`, and all 20 `^VIX` sessions 2026-09-21..10-16 equalled the close D2a ingested from FMP to the cent. Futures fail identically (D1's Brent BZX6 call, 2026-09-14, alert `d5b9e263`, cured by `exchange=NYMEX`), but that finding was recorded only in `ops/connector_tools.yaml`, which no routine reads at call time, so this bullet is the copy routines actually see. It is the almost-certain cause of D1's `^VIX` history failures on 2026-10-15 and 2026-10-18 — INFERRED, not observed, because neither run recorded the arguments it sent (the alert's parameter list names no `exchange`, and the 10-15 run's `get_price_snapshot` read succeeded, which itself requires `exchange='CBOE'`, so the omission was specific to the history call, whose tool description invites it with "omit to use SMART"): the only sentence in D1's text that ever told it to pass `exchange='CBOE'` sat in the PARK ALLOCATION CALL evidence bullet deleted at the 2026-10-15 park retirement (`bigquery/256`), and the four D1 runs before it (10-11..10-14) read the IBKR VIX bar without incident. **So when `get_price_history` answers "Details currently unavailable" for an index, future or futures option, re-issue the call ONCE with the native `exchange` before treating it as an outage, a fallback trigger or an alert; a CBOE index bar then carries `delayed: 900`, which is expected and not a fault.** This restores a missing PARAMETER only: it does NOT make `^VIX` a D1 evidence input (the PARK PIN CHECK still forbids gathering park evidence), and for any session `state.signal_marks_curated` already holds, read the warehouse value rather than re-pulling the bar (the `low <= close <= high` bullet above).

### Pass 2 input — q0131, 2026-10-30

_The newly removed second-pass text is retained in this verbatim input; history only._

**Rich market-data fields (use them wherever they sharpen a decision).** `get_price_snapshot` exposes far more than last/bid/ask:
- *Instrument eligibility:* `avg-90d-usd-volume` (when populated) gives dollar ADV for the Strategy B criterion-1 ≥$10M liquidity gate; it can be omitted by the API, so if absent derive ADV from `get_price_history` (volume × close). `misc-statistics` gives 13/26/52-week high/low.
- *Pre-event-rally / momentum context (B sub-pattern 3 + criterion-2 disproportion):* `year-to-date-change` + the 52-week range (`misc-statistics`) + returns derived from `get_price_history` quantify how much of a move was a pre-print rally already absorbing the narrative. (The `cumulative-perf-*` fields are ETF/fund-oriented and come back empty for most single stocks — do not rely on them for equities.)
- *Volatility context:* `implied-vol`, `implied-volatility-percentile`, `historical-vol` gauge whether a post-event move is large relative to the name's own vol regime.
- *Options analytics (A/C options theses):* `implied-vol`, `option-midpoint-iv`, `option-volume`, `option-open-interest`, `underlying-today/avg-option-volume`. The connector supplies both the data to BUILD and size the options thesis AND crafts the resulting order (single-leg + OPT–OPT combos).

- **AN INDEX (`security_type='IND'`), FUTURE OR FUTURES-OPTION CALL MUST PASS THE CONTRACT'S NATIVE `exchange` — OMITTED, IT DEFAULTS TO SMART, WHICH DOES NOT ROUTE THEM, AND THE CONNECTOR ANSWERS WITH A NON-SPECIFIC "Details currently unavailable" THAT READS LIKE AN OUTAGE (added 2026-10-19, interactive owner session triaging the 2026-10-19 alert emails, closing D1's `routine_run_warning` `ibkr_vix_history_unavailable`, alert `6c7a05d0-1392-42e0-baa8-11cb98745502`).** Take `exchange` verbatim from the instrument's `search_contracts` row (for a future or futures option, from the exchange its futures-search or `get_option_parameters` result reports) (`^VIX` contract_id 13455763 and SPX 416904 are both `CBOE`) and pass it on every `get_price_history` AND `get_price_snapshot` call for that instrument. **So when `get_price_history` answers "Details currently unavailable" for an index, future or futures option, re-issue the call ONCE with the native `exchange` before treating it as an outage, a fallback trigger or an alert; a CBOE index bar then carries `delayed: 900`, which is expected and not a fault.** This restores a missing PARAMETER only: it does NOT make `^VIX` a D1 evidence input (the PARK PIN CHECK still forbids gathering park evidence), and for any session `state.signal_marks_curated` already holds, read the warehouse value rather than re-pulling the bar (the `low <= close <= high` bullet above). *(Pre-2026-10-30 history and evidence for this rule: `ops/plan_history/00_preamble.md` § H-PRE-32.)*

## H-PRE-33 — FMP news/secFilings ARE PLANGATED FOR CONTENT ON THIS TIER — a sourceSELECTION fact, not a subscription proble

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 547) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

**FMP `news`/`secFilings` ARE PLAN-GATED FOR CONTENT ON THIS TIER — a source-SELECTION fact, not a subscription problem, so stop re-discovering it on a metered call every cycle (added 2026-09-14, W5 SPEC-DEFECT NOTICE INTAKE, closing M3's `fmp_tier_fallback_not_default`).** `news` is a measured whole-tool `ACCESS DENIED` (`ops/connector_tools.yaml`'s `news` entry, 2026-08-24/26 — "Route every news-shaped need to WebSearch/Tavily"). `secFilings` carries no such note in that manifest yet, but M3's 2026-09-01 run measured it `ACCESS DENIED` on all 7 tickers tried (`search-by-symbol` for UBER, GOOGL, RTX, GEV, AMZN, ISRG, DIS — `ops.web_calls` session `75dfa429`); the same session logs a successful `sec.gov`/issuer-IR `web_fetch` for 5 of those 7 (UBER, GOOGL, GEV, DIS, RTX), with zero SEC/IR fetch failures recorded anywhere in the session — AMZN and ISRG carry no matching SEC/IR fetch in this session's log, which this record states as a gap in what got logged, not as evidence EDGAR failed for them. FMP's earnings-transcript tool is separately `use: unused` in that manifest and already excluded from routine text — do not re-admit it. This is the same finding D2's 2026-08-09 GEV research-deferral adjudication reached for transcripts specifically (`events.decision_log` entry `9f6e4fa3`): issuers publish transcripts and filings free on their own IR sites and on SEC EDGAR, and an FMP plan-tier gate on THIS content class does not change that availability. **For filing-, transcript-, and news-CONTENT lookups specifically (as opposed to a structured price/quote/market-cap/calendar data POINT, where the "Shared rules referenced across prompts" Source-order rule below still sends you to FMP first), try SEC EDGAR (`sec.gov`/`data.sec.gov` via `WebFetch`) or the issuer's own IR site FIRST, falling back to Tavily search/extract — do not spend a metered call on `mcp__FMP__news`/`mcp__FMP__secFilings` to rediscover a denial they are already known to return.** This does not reorder Operating_Protocols.md §11 MARKET CAP BASIS's own secFilings-then-EDGAR sequence for its narrow shares-outstanding lookup, a different question its 2026-08-30 correction already governs on its own evidence.

## H-PRE-34 — The 15 arguments are, in order: review_id, review_type, strategy, role, review_date, cycle_number, verdict, t

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 726) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

  The 15 arguments are, in order: `review_id`, `review_type`, `strategy`, `role`, `review_date`, `cycle_number`, `verdict`, `theater_check`, `weaknesses`, `artifact_path`, `body_md`, `expected_sha256`, `source_commit_sha`, `queue_event_id`, `superseded_by`. Use `role='attacker'` or `'orchestrator'`; a normal transcript uses `NULL` for the final argument. `queue_event_id` is the exact pending/attacker-complete queue transition consumed by this run. Quote each string argument once as a GoogleSQL literal; the angle-bracket labels are placeholders, not text to submit.
- **VERIFY — do not assume.** Construct the transcript once as the canonical Unicode value and compute the lowercase SHA-256 of its exact UTF-8 bytes before writing. Call `ops.sp_write_adversarial_review(...)` with that value as `p_expected_sha256`; it rejects a mismatched body, stores `content_sha256` / `body_bytes`, and returns the inserted `event_id`. Then independently read that exact returned row and require `TO_HEX(SHA256(CAST(body_md AS BYTES))) = content_sha256 = <pre-write hash>` and `body_bytes = BYTE_LENGTH(body_md)`. A `LENGTH()` check alone is **not** sufficient — it counts characters while the canonical check is bytes and it cannot see a substitution that preserves length. If any check fails, the literal construction corrupted the text: append a corrected replacement row before logging completion. Never create a tracked repo file merely to obtain this hash. An OS-temporary scratch file is permitted only when necessary to retain raw UTF-8 bytes; hash those raw bytes and remove the file immediately after a successful readback. **HASH THE BYTES YOU WILL SUBMIT, NOT "THE FILE" — a scratch file's trailing newline is a byte too (added 2026-08-26 by AR_orc, from AR_att's own finding the same evening, `ops.alerts` 23c5a200).** Editors and file writes routinely append a final `\n` that the pasted literal then omits, so the file digest and the submitted body differ by exactly one byte. On 2026-08-26 an AR_att per-entry sub-agent hashed its scratch file whole, pasted the body without that newline, and `sp_write_adversarial_review` rejected the write on its server-side digest check — the guardrail working exactly as designed (no corrupt row landed), but it costs a full re-write of a long transcript to diagnose and it recurs for **any** routine that hashes a file and submits an inline literal. So: decide once whether the body ends with a newline, make the hashed bytes and the text between the `r'''` delimiters agree on it, and re-hash after any edit to either.
- **If a correction is needed later — APPEND, never `UPDATE`. (Rule REVERSED 2026-08-06 by `bigquery/143_adversarial_review_correction_path.sql`; the old rule said the opposite, so ignore any older copy you may have memorized.)** `events.*` is append-only (§21), and `events.adversarial_reviews` now **has a `superseded_by` column**, so it works exactly like `events.decision_log`. Call `ops.sp_write_adversarial_review(...)` with a complete replacement body and `p_superseded_by` set to the **`event_id` of the row it replaces**; reproduce every other field, not just the fixed one. Leave the bad row completely untouched: its own `superseded_by` stays `NULL` forever, and it remains in the table for audit. **Never `UPDATE`, never `DELETE`, and never write `superseded_by` onto the OLD row** — the pointer runs replacement → obsolete, not the reverse. Readers exclude the row that is *named*, via `state.adversarial_reviews_current`; a `WHERE superseded_by IS NULL` filter is the **wrong** predicate and would keep the corrupted row while dropping your fix.
  - Because `INSERT` is not a watched statement type, a correct repair raises **no** `append_only_violation` alert at all. If you see one for this table, something did an in-place `UPDATE` — that is **no longer sanctioned** and is a genuine violation to investigate, not a routine disposition.
  - Still verify after the procedure call: the returned new `event_id` must read back with `TO_HEX(SHA256(CAST(body_md AS BYTES))) = content_sha256 = <pre-write hash>`. A correction that is itself corrupted is just another bad row.
  - `cycle_number` is a **different** thing and is not a correction: a genuine re-review of the same artifact is a NEW row at a HIGHER `cycle_number`, with `superseded_by` left `NULL`. Both cycles are real history. Use `superseded_by` only when the stored text failed to faithfully mirror the artifact it was written from.

### Pass 2 input — q0201, 2026-10-30

_The newly removed second-pass text is retained in this verbatim input; history only._

## H-PRE-35 — ALERT MESSAGE LITERALS — the message IS the dedup key, and a PROSE template is the least deterministic place i

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 762) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

**ALERT MESSAGE LITERALS — the message IS the dedup key, and a PROSE template is the least deterministic place it can live (added 2026-09-07, prose message-stability sweep).** `ops.sp_raise_alert_once` dedups on `WHERE NOT resolved AND category = in_category AND message = in_message` — exact string equality on the message, and nothing else; the payload is never compared (`bigquery/10_observability.sql`). This class has now been swept three times and each pass found a shape the previous one structurally could not see: `bigquery/205` (2026-08-31) swept MOVING VALUES inside SQL-authored messages, `bigquery/227` (2026-09-06) swept `STRING_AGG` ORDERING inside SQL-authored messages, and `scripts/check_alert_message_stability.py` machine-checks the second of those — **but it parses `bigquery/*.sql` only.** The templates in THIS file are different in kind: their message is rendered by a ROUTINE (a memoryless LLM session) at run time, so nothing mechanical sees them at all and the drift arrives through prose rather than arithmetic. Four rules, all of which a raise site must satisfy:
  1. **No measurement in the message.** No dollar figure, count, percentage, elapsed-day count, row count, live value or date-of-observation. Every one of those moves while the condition persists, so `_once` never matches its own open row and degenerates into a plain `sp_raise_alert` — a fresh unresolved row and a fresh email on EVERY run. Put them in the payload, which is not part of the key, and say "see payload for …" in the message. (A date IS legitimate where it is part of the condition's IDENTITY rather than a measurement of it — `catchup_refire_blocked`'s `<as_of>` and `unlanded_completed_run`'s `<run_date>` name WHICH miss, and correctly give one open row per miss.)
  2. **No free-form placeholder.** A placeholder that reads as an instruction to compose — `<object + error>`, `<strategy/donor identifiers>`, `<message stating live_value vs expected_value from the view>` — is rendered fresh by every session and no two renderings match. Substitute NAMED FIELDS into a FIXED sentence; add nothing.
  3. **A multi-valued placeholder must have a pinned rendering.** State it at the site: DISTINCT values, sorted ASCENDING, joined by a single comma with NO spaces. An unpinned list is the prose form of the `STRING_AGG`-without-a-total-ORDER-BY defect `bigquery/227` fixed, and it permutes for exactly the same reason.
  4. **Never write `''` to escape an apostrophe.** GoogleSQL does not accept it: it reads the two literals as adjacent and fails with *"concatenated string literals must be separated by whitespace or comments"*, so the CALL does not raise the alert — it errors. Use `\'`, or rewrite the sentence to avoid the apostrophe (preferred here, since a routine transcribing a backslash is one more thing to get wrong). Three sites carried this on 2026-09-07 — `connector_reauth_needed` and both `model_of_record_synced` derivatives — while D3's original of that same message had `\'` correct all along, which is how the copies were caught.

## H-PRE-36 — PRICELEVEL CRITERION DRAFTING RULE — record the cumdividend reference date, or the level silently rots (added

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 776) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

  **PRICE-LEVEL CRITERION DRAFTING RULE — record the cum-dividend reference date, or the level silently rots (added 2026-08-23; `ops.alerts` `070cc2f0`).** Whenever a thesis records an invalidation criterion or a convergence target as a PRICE LEVEL, the entry-time `invalidation_status` JSON must also carry **`price_level_ref_date`** — the ISO date of the close/print the level was READ FROM. This is an ADDITIVE key written once at entry alongside the criteria, so it does not touch, re-word, or re-derive any criterion and does not disturb their immutability; a later row echoes it forward byte-identical exactly like every other field (it lives INSIDE `invalidation_status`, so the existing full-row echo obligation already carries it — no writer site needs a new field in its column list). **Write the level itself as a `$`-prefixed figure with exactly TWO decimals** (`$130.00`, not `$130`; `$58.22`, not `58.2`). That is not cosmetic: `state.price_level_criterion_drift` identifies a price level by the two-decimal shape, because loosening it to bare integers re-admits every integer-dollar fundamental in the book (`$2B damages`, `$200B backlog`, `$7B run-rate`). A round level written without decimals is invisible to the detector — D:RTX's dropped `stock breaks $130 on heavy volume` is exactly the shape that would be missed. **Why it is required:** a price level is a CUM-DIVIDEND observation, and the series it will later be compared against drops by every subsequent ex-dividend — so without the reference date nothing downstream can compute the like-for-like line, and the mechanical netting degrades into a remember-to. B:MSCI's 550.79 was the **2026-07-24** closing low while the position opened 2026-07-27, so even the entry date is not a safe proxy: `state.price_level_criterion_drift` (`bigquery/197`) must fall back to it and reports `reference_date_declared = FALSE` to say the number it produced is a LOWER BOUND, not the truth. Strategy D positions are unaffected in practice — D is no-stop by design and its criteria are fundamental — so this binds Strategy B's price-level criteria and convergence targets, and any future strategy that draws a line on price.

### Pass 2 input — q0215, 2026-10-30

_The newly removed second-pass text is retained in this verbatim input; history only._

That is not cosmetic: `state.price_level_criterion_drift` identifies a price level by the two-decimal shape, because loosening it to bare integers re-admits every integer-dollar fundamental in the book (`$2B damages`, `$200B backlog`, `$7B run-rate`). A round level written without decimals is invisible to the detector. **Why it is required:** a price level is a CUM-DIVIDEND observation, and the series it will later be compared against drops by every subsequent ex-dividend — so without the reference date nothing downstream can compute the like-for-like line, and `state.price_level_criterion_drift` (`bigquery/197`) must fall back to entry date and reports `reference_date_declared = FALSE`; that is a LOWER BOUND, not the truth. Strategy D positions are unaffected in practice — D is no-stop by design and its criteria are fundamental — so this binds Strategy B's price-level criteria and convergence targets, and any future strategy that draws a line on price. *(Pre-2026-10-30 history and evidence for this rule: `ops/plan_history/00_preamble.md` § H-PRE-36.)*

## H-PRE-37 — SUBTYPEB THRESHOLD RESTATEMENT + CRITERION COMPLETENESS KEYS — Strategy D (added 20260831 on operator instruc

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 778) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

  **SUBTYPE-B THRESHOLD RESTATEMENT + CRITERION COMPLETENESS KEYS — Strategy D (added 2026-08-31 on operator instruction; `ops.alerts` `b4d7172a`).** **This rule ORIGINATES nothing. It restates a requirement `Strategy.md` already carries and adds two recording keys.** `Strategy.md` `## Strategy D` → `### Thesis` → Subtype B already binds: "*Invalidation criteria* must be **quantitative thresholds on the trend metric**: e.g., \"growth rate falls below 15% YoY for 2 consecutive quarters\" = invalidation", and `### Entry criteria` item 4 repeats "Subtype B invalidation: quantitative threshold on the trend metric". **SCOPED TO SUBTYPE B ONLY.** Subtype A's menu is "financial metric breach, regulatory milestone missed, contractual obligation failure, or publicly-disclosed corporate action" and requires no threshold — extending one to Subtype A here would MANUFACTURE machinery a non-canonical surface may not create (Operating_Protocols.md §10 No Invented Position Caps is the codified instance of that failure). A non-canonical surface may DESCRIBE a canonical requirement; it may never originate one. **WHY IT IS RESTATED HERE:** D:CRM:2026-07-09 was filed subtype-B (`events.decision_log` `0c68c3c1`, opening line) with declared trend metric "Data-360+Agentforce ARR >=50% YoY AND cRPO low-double-digit", yet its `invalidation_3` was authored as the bare "non-GAAP op margin contracts YoY" — no threshold, and not on the declared trend metric. It fired 2026-08-26 on a **20 basis point** move (34.1% vs 34.3%) and closed a +44.6% position on an evening when every other criterion moved the RIGHT way and the equity repriced +12.8% after hours. **The exit was CORRECT and stands** — a criterion is applied literally, always. What failed was authoring, and it failed against a rule that was already binding: the canonical text sits in `Strategy.md`, which D2's thesis-construction step reaches only by reference, so the requirement was never in front of the session that had to apply it. **At entry, a Subtype-B criterion therefore states its threshold explicitly, and the `invalidation_status` JSON carries two ADDITIVE keys** — same shape and same standing as `price_level_ref_date` above: **`criterion_sensitivity`** per criterion (the threshold, band, or consecutive-period qualifier as drafted; *"none — fires on any adverse move, deliberate"* is a LEGAL and sometimes correct answer, and this rule sets no floor on tightness), and **`metric_perimeter`** (which figure the criterion names — "consolidated, issuer-reported non-GAAP" vs "organic ex-acquisition" — **REQUIRED where the name has announced or pending M&A**, recommended otherwise). `D:GEV:2026-08-03` already writes this shape unprompted (`primary_trend_metric` = "total-company organic orders growth YoY", `invalidation_threshold_pct`, `invalidation_consecutive_quarters`); copy it. Both are ADDITIVE keys written once at entry alongside the criteria, so they do not touch, re-word, or re-derive any criterion and do not disturb their immutability; a later row echoes them forward byte-identical exactly like every other field (they live INSIDE `invalidation_status`, so the existing full-row echo obligation already carries them — no writer site needs a new field in its column list). **The perimeter key is honest about its own limit:** it would NOT have saved D:CRM. Salesforce closed Informatica between thesis date and print ($456M, ~4% of revenue, enough to account for 20bp of consolidated dilution on mix alone) and the release discloses NO Informatica margin impact, so an organic margin was **not computable from the filing at all**. Its whole value is that a pending acquisition inside a consolidated-margin criterion's measurement window becomes visible at entry, while the criterion can still be drafted differently for free.

## H-PRE-38 — PROSPECTIVE ONLY — THIS RULE NEVER SOFTENS AN EXISTING CRITERION, AND A MISSING KEY IS NOT A DEFECT. Read thi

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 780) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

  **PROSPECTIVE ONLY — THIS RULE NEVER SOFTENS AN EXISTING CRITERION, AND A MISSING KEY IS NOT A DEFECT.** Read this clause before acting on the one above. It binds AUTHORING, from its own date forward. It does not modify, soften, back-fill, or excuse any criterion already recorded, on any position, open or closed. **D1's daily invalidation sweep, D2's exit second-look, M3 and M4 continue to apply every pre-existing criterion LITERALLY AS WRITTEN.** The absence of `criterion_sensitivity` or `metric_perimeter` on a position authored before this date is NOT a defect in that criterion and is NEVER grounds to decline to fire it, to re-interpret it, or to demand a threshold it does not state. This clause exists because the open D book contains criteria of exactly the bare shape that fired on CRM — measured live 2026-08-31 across 12 open D rows: `D:UBER:2026-07-09` `invalidation_3` "Uber One membership stalls/declines seq", `D:ISRG:2026-07-20` `invalidation_3` "recurring-rev decouples down from procedures", and `D:TSM:2026-07-21`/`2026-07-29` `invalidation_3` "structural AI-capex reset (...)" among them — and a drafting rule with no prospective-only clause hands a future session a textual basis to call those defective and NOT exit. **That is the exact INVERSE of the CRM defect and a strictly worse one:** CRM cost an early exit on a winner, whereas suppressing a real invalidation keeps a broken thesis alive on a strategy that has no stop-loss and, since the 2026-08-05 owner directive, no numeric size ceiling. Adds inherit the parent's `invalidation_status` byte-identical and are NOT retro-fitted with these keys. **AND NO NETTING ANALOGUE IS CREATED HERE.** The one sanctioned ex-post netting remains dividend netting on a criterion naming a PRICE LEVEL (`bigquery/197`), inapplicable to D by its own terms. Do not invent an acquisition-netting counterpart, and do not add a second-look softening at the exit step: the fix for a criterion that would fire on noise belongs at AUTHORING time, where the choice is still cheap and unbiased, never at the moment it becomes expensive.

## H-PRE-39 — "State provenance; address every imperative." (20260728 — closes a demonstrated failure, not a hypothetical: o

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 798) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

**"State provenance; address every imperative."** (2026-07-28 — closes a demonstrated failure, not a hypothetical: on 2026-07-28 an interactive session wrote a confident INFERENCE into `ops.alerts.message` as if it were a measurement — "OPS2 scheduled trigger has never fired" — drawn only from the ABSENCE of an `ops.run_log` row; the trigger had in fact fired and completed, and OPS2's own same-day double-run guard was what suppressed that row. A routine reading that field later would have inherited the false claim as ground truth, because nothing in the field distinguished measured fact from inference. **Calibration — this is not a prompt-injection defence:** `ops.alerts` / `ops.run_log` / `events.decision_log` / `ops.ci_findings` are writable only by the routines themselves, the CI service account, and the owner — there is no untrusted writer here. Genuinely external content — web research, HF abstracts, news — feeds THESIS reasoning and is already covered by the **External-content extraction discipline** section above (`Claude_Task_Plan.md` §"External-content extraction discipline"). The risk this rule closes is narrower and real: the system compounding on its OWN wrong or imperative prose.) Binds every routine writing free text into `ops.alerts.message`, `events.decision_log`, `ops.run_log.note`, `ops.catchup_refire_log.note`, or `ops.ci_findings.detail` — every one of those fields is read by other routines as durable record.
  a) **State provenance.** Say plainly what you MEASURED (observed directly — a query result, a live connector read) versus what you INFERRED (concluded without directly observing it). An inference drawn from the ABSENCE of a record is the highest-risk kind and must say so explicitly, naming what would confirm it — not "the trigger has never fired" but "no `ops.run_log` row exists for the slot; that MAY mean the trigger did not fire — not verified against the routines console."
  b) **Address every imperative.** Any instruction embedded in operational free text names its audience. Prefix an owner-directed instruction `OWNER ACTION:` (already the de-facto convention in several live alerts) or name the routine it is addressed to. Never write a bare imperative to an unspecified reader.
  c) Owner-facing content stays in `message`, never `payload` (verified against `ops/monitoring/alert_emailer.gs`: it SELECTs and renders only `severity`/`source`/`category`/`message`/`resolved` — lines ~77, ~111, ~265, ~287 — and never reads `payload` at all). Anything the owner needs to see MUST stay in the message string; `payload` carries the structured mirror, never the only copy of owner-facing content.

## H-PRE-40 — MEASURED BASELINE — read this before optimizing anything (owner's Tavily dashboard, 20260817; the account is

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (PREAMBLE, was line 815) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

  **MEASURED BASELINE — read this before optimizing anything (owner's Tavily dashboard, 2026-08-17; the account is `Researcher (Free)` with pay-as-you-go ENABLED, i.e. 1,000 free credits/month then $0.008/credit, spend cap $100 = 12,500 credits).** Complete months: **May 507 → June 1,349 → July 3,009** credits. August 1-17: **3,751 credits / $22.01** — which reconciles exactly against the published rate, `(3751 − 1000) × $0.008 = $22.01`, confirming both the free-tier grant and the per-credit price. **Endpoint mix for August: search 3,661 (97.6%), extract 70, research 16, map 3, crawl 1.** Two consequences bind the parts below. **(1) SEARCH IS ESSENTIALLY THE ENTIRE BILL** — extract, research, crawl and map together are 2.4%, so effort spent shaving them is effort wasted; the levers that matter are search COUNT and search DEPTH, in that order. `tavily_research` is a real tail RISK (4-250 credits, allowlisted, unprompted) but is **not** a realized cost today at 16 credits/month — guard it, do not re-engineer around it. **(2) THE CURVE, NOT THE LEVEL, IS WHAT TO WATCH — and the cap is a DELIBERATE TRIGGER, not a hazard to engineer away (owner decision 2026-08-17).** Consumption has roughly doubled-to-tripled every month and the 12,500-credit cap is only ~2x the current run-rate, so it will likely be reached. **The owner has decided to LEAVE THE CAP AT $100 on purpose: hitting it is the signal to move to a monthly Tavily tier, which is cheaper at that volume.** The arithmetic behind that decision — PAYG costs `(credits − 1000) × $0.008`, so at 13,500 credits it equals Bootstrap's flat $100/15,000; below that PAYG is cheaper, above it Bootstrap is — means the cap and the switch-point are essentially the SAME number, which is exactly why the cap makes a good trigger. **A future session must NOT reflexively raise the cap, treat reaching it as an incident, or "fix" it by switching plans early** — switching below ~13,500 credits/month costs money. The early warning is already wired on Tavily's side: an **"Email usage alerts" threshold is enabled at 80%** (verified in Tavily Settings 2026-08-17), which fires around 11,000 credits — while PAYG is still the cheaper option — so the correct response to that email is to PROJECT month-end usage and switch tiers only if the projection clears ~13,500, never to act on the alert alone. What genuinely must not happen is reaching the hard cap *unnoticed*, since Tavily then errors mid-run — the same degradation that cost the 2026-08-17 sweep its COHR detection when FMP's cap blew. The 80% email plus `state.web_spend_month` are the two things preventing that; the cap itself is working as designed. Four binding parts, plus a documentary fifth:
